Proven absent or released/abandoned authority freezes no payload paths, but a changed sidecar still requires an authorized transition. Main updates depend on the policy loading successfully even without a held lease; the shell fast path applies only to unrelated refs/phases. The hooks authenticate transitions and do not validate drift evidence.

Typed Land/publication internals run with hooks disabled. Their lease protection is at the consumer doors: `landRunnerItem` and Workflow's `mergeWorkflowBranch` check committed authority and the explicit caller checkout before checkpoint/candidate/WAL effects. Work re-admits after its checkpoint/wait against the post-checkpoint target. Only Work's declared resolution files require an explicit target OID in `prepare`, binding those bytes to that target. Otherwise both doors let Land select the current target under its merge slot, so a second sequential Land can integrate onto the first one's result. Before publish they re-admit the actual prepared source/target, recompute scoped paths from that exact pair's merge base, and re-check current committed lease authority. A newly blocking lease refuses with the existing `documentation-cutover-lease` reason (corrupt authority retains its named refusal); a benign target advance before prepare is not itself a lease refusal. If acquire advances main after this check or during native lock admission, Land's exact-target/expected-old check prevents publication; the consumer rechecks the lease on stale and reports its named refusal if blocking, otherwise preserves Git's stale result without retry. LandRequest has no separate expected-merge-base field: the immutable prepared source/target OIDs bind that base. Scope-touching non-holder lands refuse; code-only and authenticated holder lands proceed. Corrupt authority refuses even a no-op Land. Hook coverage is not evidence for these typed doors. Owning hook proof: `test/e2e/documentation-cutover-lease-hooks.test.mjs`; owning Land boundary proofs: `test/runner/merge-land.test.mjs` and `test/workflow/units-runner.test.mjs`.

## 8. Scoped commit and sync

2026-10-20 (typed-operation performance): the existing storage-format probe batches object/ref formats in one Git invocation. ScopedCommit reuses its pre-staging dirty-path observation for the rollback inventory (no intervening worktree mutation), and reads the resulting immutable commit OID/tree/parents together. Land checkout enumeration reuses stamp-validated facts only when gitdir, common-dir and worktree all match through the same checkout-identity predicate; other worktrees still resolve their own config. Recovery’s old-index digest and expected next-index entries derive from the same canonical stage-entry read. These facts persist beyond the operation only as the per-process entry, which every use revalidates against the ConfigStamp; post-hook refresh, per-child topology/config/authority validation, WAL barriers and all exact-old/recovery guards remain required. The existing E3 `scoped-census` mode traces paired typed/legacy operations, listing Git exec arguments and exclusive holder-lock acquisitions separately from borrow validations; prepare and publish times are reported separately. The suite uses `scoped-census --observer wrapper` to count direct Git launches without an external tracing tool; it does not measure internal Git children or locks. The default host census requires strace and fails if tracing is unavailable. Census timings under strace are diagnostic, not E3 verdicts.

**Literal selected paths.** Scope declarations keep the RUL84 grammar, but a selected filename is a literal path, not another declaration. Star, tab, newline and surrounding whitespace round-trip as escaped UTF-8 JSON strings in the ScopedCommit WAL `paths` array. Staging uses `--literal-pathspecs` and NUL-terminated stdin. Invalid UTF-8 is refused with the existing lossless encoded diagnostic; NUL, absolute paths and traversal segments are refused. Resolver grants still require a path the declaration grammar can name exactly.

**Store exclusion and legacy event authority.** ScopedCommit omits `.fgos` and its descendants even under `**`, tracked or untracked, without refusing the other selected paths. Only explicit `legacyEvents.exactPaths` (the closed legacy event layout) or the existing cutover grant admits store paths. Every legacy event commit, including additions and modifications, validates main-checkout 50 borrowing through the same request validation as Checkpoint; the deletion hook checks the same closed layout and main-50 authority. Unknown recovery operation ids are typed errors; only a persisted pending intent can be abandoned before attempt.

**Unit record and continuation.** Execution persists the native pre-producer baseline and original writer identity, Sync operation id before start, and the exact pending ScopedCommit request before commit. Resume carries current verified custody into native inspection/recovery and reuses that recorded intent; it never manufactures a new pre-producer baseline. A writing attempt without one requires a new run. A known rejected Sync commit may be retried only through `Sync.continue` with a persisted fresh `nextOperationId`: Git carries the existing merge's recorded state into that new WAL operation, retaining the original terminal cause and resolver bytes. Preparation interruptions resume the same id/pin; unknown commit effects are observed, never replayed. Manual completion/abort settles and compacts the old Sync chain; completion is reported as outside rather than attributed to Git. The Unit adapter requests the existing Sync abort for a rejected **clean-start** merge before returning refusal; rejection while concluding a conflicted merge keeps it open. The baseline head advances only from the native Sync receipt/inspection, leaving its pre-existing dirty-path exclusions intact.

Before producer, check trusted linked worktree/custody, no foreign staged entries even in scope, no foreign MERGE_HEAD; capture source baseline, existing dirty paths/content and ref. Worker may not stage or commit. After producer, exact authorized path selection includes both rename sides/deletions; revalidate authority/state and stage only selected literal paths. On failure unstage only this operation's entries and preserve bytes/foreign changes/cause. No-op differs from failure. Verify actual committed set/tree after hooks. If hook changed it, return `committed-but-contract-violation` with real OID; preserve effect, do not retry producer as though nothing happened. Resume commit-only retains captured intent/custody.

Sync merges pinned target OID into producer using no-commit/no-ff. `prepare` leaves a clean owned merge in `prepared` state under continuous source custody so Work can verify before `continue` commits it, or `abort` restores it after a red check. Same-id preparation observes the existing operation and never commits it. `start` retains its existing immediate clean-commit contract for Unit sync. Source-side non-event tracked `.fgos` stays source-side; workers receive no store grants. Clean sync records two exact parents.

2026-10-20 (phase 07 unit 13): `Sync.prepare` accepts the consumer's literal `files` declarations with the same validation and application as Land's (§9): they replace the store restore for their own paths, are written and staged after the merge, are part of the request digest (a same-id request with other declarations is `operation-id-reused`), and abort restores them like every other staged merge path. `start` (Unit sync) declares nothing, so source-side `.fgos` stays source-side there. Catchup (`performCatchUp`) is Work's consumer: it reads the read-only `merge-tree` of the pinned target and the branch, reports a conflict outside `.fgos` as the verb's `conflict` before any checkout or Sync exists (so no merge bytes remain for Worktree removal to preserve), and declares the store rule Land uses (§9 unit 11) — `merge=union` `.fgos` conflicts and stale `.fgos` paths keep the target's bytes — plus the merge result's bytes for every other `.fgos` path the merge changes, so a catchup merge commit never reads as the branch reverting the target's store. A branch's own store write is left on the branch for Land to refuse as `fgos-write-rejected`. A merge Git refuses outright (G-067) returns Git's own refusal text as the reason.

**G-071 effective (C6), not A's “abort all out-of-scope”.** Only the operation's scopable conflicts receive a **temporary** resolver grant; original producer `writeScope` remains unchanged; non-event `.fgos`, unscopable and forbidden paths do not gain grants. Continue refuses leftover conflict markers (seven or more `<`, `>` or vertical-bar characters followed by whitespace/end; skip binary/non-regular files) without staging, and validates parents/MERGE_HEAD/index/custody, detects manual completion/abort as done/not-started/lost, stages only owned conflict paths. Hook rejection retains cause. Sync-only without conflict work never spawns a producer.

**G-202 frozen by owner decision Q5 (2026-10-17).** Catalogue §7 Q16 recorded catchup-vs-sync as an open design decision; the owner closed it. Catchup uses the same isolated scoped resolver **when custody exists** (verified `dispatch-cwd` lease, §6); without custody it aborts only its own merge. Land candidate conflicts **abort**. A scoped resolver session without custody is forbidden (no resolver on a worktree the runner does not own). The decision came from the owner, not from the frozen catalogue.

2026-10-19 (phase 05 re-review): checkout resources include the worktree's realpath directory identity (dev, inode and birthtimeNs), using the exact custody lock path. A recreated directory at the same path never inherits the dead checkout's chains. Legacy path-only chains remain readable; a binding to a different custody lock is foreign. Admission closes foreign Intent/Prepared chains as `CommitRejected{foreign-checkout-abandoned}` and foreign TargetAttempt/SyncState/SyncCommitAttempt chains as `CommitRejected{foreign-checkout-effect-unknown}` (replay remains recovery-required). It preserves existing terminal causes, releases their pins and attempts ledger compaction without touching the current index or replaying effects. Only outcome-append errors block admission. After the outcome is durable, pin-release/compaction errors produce `checkout-compaction-deferred` warnings and admission retries cleanup next time. WorktreeRemove and WorktreeMove source resources also carry the existing directory custody key, so live checkout attempts exclude lifecycle changes; checkout admission refuses active Create/Remove/Move path resources, filtering by operation kind so legacy checkout chains still receive foreign classification. A foreign chain with a durable outcome cannot block the current checkout even if its compaction is deferred; its id cannot be replayed by the new checkout.

Under verified checkout custody, admission and inline pre-attempt failures close Intent/Prepared chains with `AbandonedBeforeAttempt`; recovery unstages their exact paths only when HEAD equals the baseline and the complete staged set equals those paths. `ScopedCommit.recover({operationId})` is observe-only: exact ordered parent(s) plus the intended tree prove a landed commit. For the newest checkout chain, exact parents plus the verbatim request message also prove it; a different tree records `contract_violation:true`. Admission excludes later cooperative checkout attempts while a chain is open; retained peer baselines are checked conservatively without using record age. Recovery uses one bounded `git log -g -n 1024` call on the recorded branch, stopping candidate processing at the baseline barrier after a candidate entry. It exhausts exact parent/tree matches before message-only matches. A candidate must be reachable from current HEAD; a reflog-only (ABA/reset-away) match keeps the chain open recovery-required and names its OID as evidence. SyncIntent records the symbolic branch; changed branches refuse unresolved SyncCommitAttempt recovery, and legacy records without a branch refuse with `sync-reference-unrecorded`. Without immutable commit proof, unchanged baseline HEAD, an absent native index lock and quiescent custody permit terminal `CommitRejected{commit-effect-unknown}` (Sync: `sync-commit-effect-unknown`); unstage is limited to the operation's paths. Other ambiguous states remain recovery-required. No attempt is replayed. Same-id replay returns the recorded cause, including unknown effects. New Sync refuses detached HEAD as `sync-detached-head-refused`. Compacted Sync replay matches the request digest using the recorded reference (or None for older receipts), independently of the live branch, and returns its recorded terminal state (`conflict-aborted`, `hook-rejected`, or the unknown cause), never a generic `not-started`.

An observed Sync hook rejection records `CommitRejected{hook-rejected}` and retains the open merge and pin until explicit abort. Abort restores recorded clean merged paths in both index and worktree to HEAD (including removal of target-only additions), preserving resolver bytes only on recorded conflict paths; if no conflict state was durably recorded, all merge paths return to HEAD. Foreign staged paths still refuse. Terminal checkout chains use Publication's WAL compaction ledger after pin release, compacting terminal chains immediately; compact receipts preserve id/request/authority bindings and reconstruct committed files from immutable commits. Baseline-dirty in-scope files changed by the producer remain excluded and are reported as `preExistingInScope`. Rollback captures bytes only for dirty, untracked, ignored or absent declared paths; clean tracked files restore from HEAD with their original permission metadata reapplied. Skip-worktree entries are excluded from the clean inventory. SyncState optionally retains pre-merge permission metadata for clean merged regular files; older states remain readable without it. Restore failures do not stop independent path restores; after all attempts they report `commit-worktree-restore-required`. Git's representation/configuration limits (including submodules and symlink emulation) remain outside an exact-content guarantee. Once an outcome is recorded, a failed compaction returns the actual receipt with `warnings: ["checkout-compaction-deferred: …"]`; the next checkout admission retries compaction. Recovery's outcome-only API likewise preserves the recorded outcome and retained WAL. Unsupported literal control/star paths refuse before staging or Intent.

## 9. Land: candidate, verification, publication

2026-10-20 (phase 06 unit 1; **not release-ready**): Rust `prepare_land`/`publish_land`, addon `GitLand`, and Node `createGitLand` implement prepare, evidence binding, publication and M4 receipt replay. `prepare({operationId, request})` owns the encoded destination merge-slot 20 for the native session; callers pass only lower held ranks (an already held slot is refused as `land-slot-already-held`, anything above 20 as `lock-order-violation`, before effects). The candidate is created through Worktree's detached allocation/WAL door under 40. Construction and consumer verification retain only slot 20, following steps 2-3 below; 40/50/60 are absent during verification. `publish(evidence)` reacquires 40 then 50, validates all three commit pins/types, exact candidate HEAD/index/status, bound OIDs/tree digest and nonempty opaque consumer evidence, then records `land-verified → target-attempt → outcome`. The candidate status check covers HEAD, index and tracked files; untracked verification or setup output cannot enter the frozen tree and does not block publication, while a staged addition or tracked edit refuses as `land-candidate-dirty`. The tree digest is the canonical SHA-256 digest of the immutable tree OID. Consumer-declared file bytes are applied before the candidate is frozen, with literal staging and symlink traversal refusal. Git does not execute or certify verification commands.

The own-file clean gate combines literal committed paths with the canonical declared footprint; absent footprint is fail-safe, `.fgos` never counts, and status disables rename folding so both staged rename ends count. The existing Refs ignored/incoming collision check is shared with Land. A colliding ignored non-store path refuses before CAS; an ignored store collision preserves bytes by leaving checkout reconciliation pending. Successful dirty non-overlapping publication reports `checkout: pending` (checkout-lagging), never synced. The receipt has `operationId` and `consumerSettlement: notOwnedByGit`; successful same-id replay returns its established receipt even after external ref ABA. Receipt persistence releases the session slot as well as 40/50; `close()` releases an abandoned session without erasing evidence. An unresolved same-id attempt is refused without any re-effect. Production caller migration remains deferred to phase 07. The M4 Node contract fixture demonstrates the consumer's unique `(repoIdentity, operationId, settlementKind)` identity and query-before-retry rule; it is not evidence that Work/Event Store have adopted that mechanism.

2026-10-20 (phase 06 unit 2): the existing `GitLand`/`createGitLand` port adds `recover({operationId, consumerAcknowledgement?}, held?) → {receipt, warnings}` through Rust `recover_land`; no separate repair core or production caller is introduced. Recovery acquires destination 20, admin 40, main 50, validates quiescence, then takes recovery 80 last. An existing native session excludes it. Foreign writer/repository/binding, corrupt chains and absent objects fail closed. Before TargetAttempt, recovery records `abandonedBeforeAttempt`; after TargetAttempt it never reissues the target CAS, adopts reflog-only candidates or rolls back another writer. A missing receipt at the recorded candidate can be completed with `effectObservedOnly`, preserving that attribution in compacted replay. Prepared pin=old can be CAS-repaired to the recorded candidate after object/parent checks; unknown native ref/index locks remain untouched.

Before publication without an ignored store collision, `land-verified` records bounded per-path old/next fingerprints, the old index tree/file fingerprint and the expected next stage-entry digest (including unrelated staged changes), using the existing no-follow snapshot primitive. Recovery finishes an exactly captured old checkout under 50; it completes an already-next checkout after read-back, and reports unrelated dirt as pending. Mixed files, missing fingerprints, unreadable/unsupported path types, index drift and the interrupted `read-tree` shape with an unowned `index.lock` return `recovery-required`, preserving files/index/lock. This unit does not manufacture native-lock ownership from a matching ref or index tree; the owned dead-child quarantine machinery remains the native-repair unit's responsibility (§11). Native `read-tree` is one Git child. In addition to the production filesystem checkpoints, isolated Linux/x86_64 ptrace tests SIGKILL its actual descendants after native index-lock creation, main-file writing, index writing and index installation; the files-next/index-old/lock-present shapes preserve the exact native residue. A direct constructed-state regression also checks that unknown lock contents are never adopted.

2026-10-20 (unit 5 recovery fixes): exact-old index proof uses canonical `ls-files --stage -z` digest plus index tree and per-path fingerprints, tolerating stat refresh. LandVerified explicitly records ignored-store reconciliation skips. Unresolved recovery checks main native state, including `index.lock` and `MERGE_HEAD`, even when the destination is no longer checked out; terminal and compacted receipt replay precede that check. Prepare/publish check native main state only when the destination is checked out there. Post-attempt recovery rechecks writer equality against the chain inspected under destination slot 20.

2026-10-20 (phase 06 review fixes): two-tree reconciliation preserves staged modifications and staged additions outside the own-file set; staged overlap refuses before CAS. Recovery captures the expected next index stage-entry digest and can finish or recognize the same preserved staged state. Legacy proofs without next entries retain their clean-index expectation. WAL outcome `pendingStoreCollision` distinguishes an untouched checkout from reconciled checkout with tolerated dirt (`pending`); both map to receipt `Checkout::Pending`. Prepare validates declared file paths before allocating candidates; later failures retain the candidate path and Worktree operation id in bounded error diagnostics. Under the destination slot, admission closes the same writer/authority's pre-attempt Land chains as `abandonedBeforeAttempt`, retaining pins/candidate; a different writer requires named recovery. Finished same-id replay precedes slot acquisition, and lost replay retains the stale error and receipt. Publish checks WAL phase before live ref/candidate checks; an attempted identity requires recovery even when the ref moved. Refs and Land share post-attempt checkout-facts/CAS classification: unchanged or candidate tip after a failed native CAS requires recovery evidence, only a third-party tip is Loss. Already-merged source tips refuse a fresh Land identity.

2026-10-20 (phase 07 unit 11): Land never interprets `.fgos`, decision rows or merge attributes. Work computes its trusted-side resolutions before `prepare` from a read-only `git merge-tree --write-tree` of the exact source and target OIDs and passes them as declared `files`: a conflict confined to `merge=union` `.fgos` paths, and every `.fgos` path the branch did not write since its take point keep the target's bytes. Any other conflict is the verb's `conflict`; any remaining `.fgos` change is `fgos-write-rejected` before a candidate exists. Declarations are bound to the target they were read against. Only declared resolution files require an explicit `target`; otherwise Land selects the current target under its merge slot, and both runner and Workflow doors re-admit the actual prepared source/target before publication (§7). The repo-invariant checks run in the candidate with the item's verify.

2026-10-22 (sequence counters): the Git component owns the counter refs Identity claims sequence numbers from (`refs/fgos/seq/<kind>`, `packages/git/rust/src/sequence.rs`, identity spec section 4a). `sequence_tip` reads one counter (a remote `ls-remote`, then a fetch of its single commit when the object is not local, or the repository's own ref when it has no `origin`); `sequence_push` replaces it with a new parentless commit on the empty tree whose message is the number, with `--force-with-lease=<ref>:<old sha>` (`<ref>:` while absent) on a remote and `update-ref <ref> <new> <old>` locally, answering `Claimed` or `Stale`; any other failure (no network, a refused push that is not a lost lease) is an error, never `Stale`. The claim commit carries a caller-chosen claimant as its author name: two claims of one number in the same second would otherwise build the very same commit, and the forge would accept both. No WAL and no holder lock: the compare-and-swap is the whole protocol, a lost lease changes nothing, and the retry loop belongs to Identity. The decision-id collision resolution that Work declared into Land (renumbering a branch's decision file at merge) is deleted: a number is claimed once, before the commit that uses it, so two branches never hold the same one.

2026-10-20 (phase 07 unit 12): every runner merge (root into the trunk, leaf into `fgw/<root>`, a nested root's sync into its parent, a promoted member into its integration branch) is one consumer sequence, `landRunnerItem` in `src/runner/merge.mjs`, on this Land transaction. The destination slot is only Land's, keyed by the full destination ref; the verbs' caller-held slot, the short-branch key, the ephemeral merge worktree with its own ref CAS and the main-checkout merge commit are deleted. The `MERGE_HEAD` refusal and the no-wait main precheck apply only when the destination is the branch the main checkout holds; a destination another linked checkout holds refuses as `foreign-target-checkout` (step 4) and the verb reports it as an error naming that target. Work event files a checkpoint is due for are committed by the temporary Work adapter's Checkpoint/ScopedCommit under main 50 before `prepare` pins the target; nothing is staged or swept around Land. An already-merged destination the main checkout does not hold is verified in a disposable detached checkout created through the Worktree admin door and removed through Worktree removal. After Work durably records the delivery (`approve`) or the sync decision (`sync-root`), the verb passes that record as `consumerAcknowledgement` to `recover`; a retained candidate is reported as a warning and never negates the land. Red, conflicted and promote candidates are still retained until a Work acknowledgement exists for them.

2026-10-20 (phase 07 unit 14): every `landRunnerItem` result whose operation Land journalled carries Work's settlement identity: `delivered` for a publication, `abandoned` for a result that published nothing (red verify, a candidate conflict, `main-checkout-dirty-mid-merge`, an unclassified failure). After Work durably records the result, the verb acknowledges it through the same `recover` door: approve's move to `blocked` (`work-blocked:<id>:<seq>`), an engine decision for a sync-root result that published nothing, and promote-to-component's one summary decision for every member, merged or not. Recovery closes the pre-attempt chain as `abandonedBeforeAttempt` and the existing cleanup admission removes its candidate, pins and journal. No identity is attached when Land journalled nothing under the id (a refusal before its intent), so there is nothing to release. A Land error that the verb rethrows (a lock refusal at publication) records no Work result and keeps its candidate.

2026-10-21 (phase 07 unit 24, review fixes): Recovery's `inspectOperation` refuses an id with no journal, live or compacted, with the exact cause `git-operation-unknown`. Approve records its Land operation id as a decision before Land starts; when a retry finds that decision and Land reports `git-operation-unknown` for it, no attempt exists under the id, so the retry reuses it (no second decision is written). Any other inspection failure still refuses. A recorded operation that is non-terminal at `land-verified` or `target-attempt` refuses the retry and names `fgos doctor --fix`, whose registered fix `git-land-recovery` lists pending operations through Recovery and reconciles each Land one through `recover_land` under its own locks: pre-attempt chains close `abandonedBeforeAttempt` whoever runs it (§10 rule 4b), attempted ones only from the writer that started them, and the fix names that writer otherwise; it releases no candidate, which stays until Work acknowledges it. In `landRunnerItem` an error in the publish phase that the consumer cannot classify records no Work result: the verb throws `land-effect-unknown` naming the same fix, and never claims an `abandoned` settlement for it. Post-land drift detection runs after the publication's error handling; its failure is reported in the result's `postLand.error` beside the land.

**Cleanup admission is precise:** no acknowledgement means retain candidate, all three pins and WAL, even after a successful receipt. A nonempty `consumerAcknowledgement` is the consumer's assertion that its unique `(repoIdentity, operationId, settlementKind)` entry is durably acknowledged; Git continues to return `consumerSettlement: notOwnedByGit` and never writes business settlement. Cleanup requires Worktree's existing clean, not-live and custody checks. Delivered candidates additionally require a reconciled/not-applicable terminal Land receipt and externally reachable delivery proof; terminal `abandonedBeforeAttempt` and `Loss` candidates require no delivered proof because nothing was delivered. The Worktree removal WAL binds the acknowledgement identity digest and caller writer before deletion. It uses the existing journaled Worktree removal door, then exact-old base/candidate pin deletes and the existing compact ledger. Dirty/unproven candidates, pending checkouts, changed pins and compaction failures are retained with warnings without negating Land. Compacted Land retains destination, expected/next, request/writer/authority binding and terminal attribution; same-id request replay returns the receipt after candidate deletion and external ref drift. An interrupted ledger deletion resumes under the same slot/admin authority. Land request matching uses a canonical digest of the declared request and writer, independent of disposable candidate directory identity.

Consumer supplies a source OID, an optional expected target OID, merge or squash, and **policy evidence bound to the prepared source/target/tree**. Omitting `request.target` pins the current destination only after Land acquires its own full-ref slot 20; an explicit expected target retains the exact-target stale check. The prepared candidate and persisted intent always contain the resulting target OID. `prepare({operationId, request, waitMs?})` waits only for slot admission (default zero); it never retries candidate construction or a publication CAS. `publish(evidence, {waitMs?})` likewise waits only for main-checkout 50 admission (default zero; merge verbs pass their existing `--wait` budget) and never retries the publication. Both waits run inside the native port, as one attempt loop on a thread of its own, and the call settles as a promise: the event loop is never blocked, so a holder in the same process (and the verification children, timers and other Lands) keeps running while a Land waits. An attempt that cannot take its lock has made no effect. The port is `Idle`, `Pending` or `Open`: a second `prepare` or `publish` while a call is pending refuses `land-call-pending`, `prepare` while a session is open refuses `land-session-already-open`, and `close()` while a call is pending cancels it on settle (the produced session is dropped and the call rejects `land-session-closed`; a `publish` that settles after a close keeps its receipt). A worker that panics leaves the port idle and fails `land-worker-failed`. A caller that lends held locks gets one synchronous attempt: a non-zero `waitMs` with held locks refuses `land-wait-with-held-locks`, so the loan guard's synchronous invariant is untouched. The Node facade (`src/git/land.mjs`) holds no retry loop. Same-id replay matches the original declared request/writer digest, including whether an expected target was supplied, before taking the slot, even after cleanup/compaction or external destination movement.

Consumer `files` declarations may supply literal resolution bytes for candidate conflicts. Land proceeds only when every unmerged path has an exact declaration; otherwise it aborts the candidate and returns the existing conflict cause. It applies and stages these declarations before freezing the candidate commit/tree for verification. It never interprets decision rows or event meaning, and declarations for unrelated paths cannot authorize an uncovered conflict.

Work owns lineage/dependencies/approval/Iron Law; Git enforces the frozen binding and never imports Work. Workflow calls the same port. Squash creates a real one-parent commit; ordinary merge exact target/source parents; a staged squash index is not success.

Honest verification contract (not “nothing unverified is published” via tree equality):

- Consumer produces evidence bound to exact OIDs/tree (runs verify/build in the candidate worktree through Process, **outside Git**).
- Git binds that digest and refuses publish on mismatch, missing evidence, or dirty candidate (`write-tree` ≠ candidate commit tree, or digest mismatch).
- Git does **not** claim tests ran. A lying consumer can publish an unverified tree that matches the candidate. Tree proof is identity, not verification. V-VERIFY must not be marked passed by tree equality alone.

1. Reserve target merge-slot 20; inspect true topology, source/target and target checkout status/index/MERGE_HEAD. Reject foreign state.
2. Create detached operation candidate under admin 40, release admin, construct merge from pinned target. Land conflict aborts candidate only; source and target preserved. Consumer transformations of non-event `.fgos`/decision documents produce declared candidate bytes, not Git-owned state meaning.
3. Consumer verifies/builds outside 50/60. Freeze tree/evidence before and after; any changed candidate/dependency setup invalidates evidence. No post-verify strip/renumber can publish bytes that do not match the bound digest. Pin candidate/bases against normal GC.
4. Reacquire required admin 40/main 50 in order; revalidate target/source, evidence and candidate; re-run the own-file-set clean gate (G-098/G-102: union of committed diff and declared footprint, normalized; whole-repo scope for land, subtree scope for return; `.fgos` never counts; fail-safe to every non-`.fgos` path when the set is absent; a staged rename blocks when either half is own) and return `main-checkout-dirty-mid-merge` without moving the ref when it fails. Never use main dirty bytes as merge input. Detect foreign linked checkout holding target; v1 refuses publication into it unless it is an explicitly supported operation-owned checkout with custody. Never move a branch behind another index.
5. Durable intent/prepared pins and the TargetAttempt record (§10 rule 4) precede exact-old target CAS; a target CAS with an unknown outcome is never reissued. CAS loser returns stale and cannot reuse verification against a new target. When the main checkout holds the target, the winner runs non-destructive `read-tree -m -u <old destination> <candidate>` under main 50; non-overlapping dirty paths are tolerated and the checkout is left lagging per D-ADR0042 (G-099, preserved by owner decision Q7), recorded in the receipt as checkout-lagging rather than failing the land; ref success is never reported as checkout synced. No blind `reset --hard`.
6. Read back actual ref/tree/index, durably record receipt, release locks; consumer records actual `mergedSha`/`mergedInto` by operation id. Settlement failure is landed/consumer-settlement-pending, not permission to merge again. Land and cleanup remain separate. **Consumer settlement contract (M4 fold).** M4 held for an append failure known to precede its effect; its negative control showed that an append whose outcome is unknown (ack lost), retried without deduplication, produces two business settlements for one land. So the consumer's settlement is unique by `(repoIdentity, operationId, settlementKind)`; when an append outcome is unknown the consumer queries or replays that identity before any retry; a Git receipt or local success never mints a new settlement identity; cleanup runs only after durable consumer acknowledgement. Work/Event Store own this mechanism; Git states it as the precondition of its no-second-land guarantee.

Raw external Git ignores fgOS mutex. It is outside concurrency guarantees; pre/post drift is detected and preserved as recovery-required, never silently overwritten. A hook early probe is not full exclusion against raw checkout/reset/read-tree.

## 10. Journal: durability, schema and ownership

**One recovery authority: the file WAL (owner decision Q3).** Location: `<git-common-dir>/fgos/git/operations/<operation-id>/`, outside worktree/runtime scratch, shared by linked worktrees, no config/env override. Operational state for recovering effects under L7, not product event truth. Consumers still publish business history through Event Store. L1/L3 are not changed.

### Driver state root

2026-10-20 (phase 07 unit 18): `GitDriver::new` creates an absent state root through the same preparation as the `git-driver-directories` setup fix (mode 0700, empty `git-empty-hooks` mode 0500), so `fgos init`'s workspace read works before any other fgOS command has run. An existing root is only checked, never repaired here.

2026-10-19: the caller-owned trusted driver state root may contain `git-floor-verdict.json`, an optional floor-probe cache, alongside `git-empty-hooks` and temporary probe repositories. It is not WAL or operation evidence. Missing, unreadable, symlinked, inconsistent or differently keyed records cause probing again. The key binds the selected Git binary identity, host package version, probe implementation digest, floor contract, inherited `PATH`, `HOME`/`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, and config-file identities. System/global paths come from the selected Git's `git var GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM/GLOBAL` discovery, shared with publication preflight; paths are never guessed from an installation prefix. Any include/includeIf directive disables persistent reuse (including missing or inactive targets), so conditional and included files cannot escape invalidation. The recorded probe names must match the required contract set exactly, without duplicates. A cache write failure only costs a later probe; no setup default, environment switch or doctor prerequisite is introduced.

### Operation evidence

Empty `RemoteFetch`/`RemotePush.targets` is omitted during serialization, preserving historical record digests and previous-digest links. Publication push first advertises the remote tip (one extra network round-trip); its target tuple is keyed by the local publication resource name, with the remote old tip and local next tip. The tuple describes the transfer against that publication resource, not a local ref CAS. Non-event transfers require complete per-target details.

Do **not** run a dual-write protocol. Pins under `refs/fgos/git-operations/<id>/` are reachability only. The pin is written before the target CAS and is not the receipt; a multi-ref transaction is never assumed crash-atomic (§4). E1 decides whether git `core.fsync=objects,reference` would suffice for a **future** ref-only journal (B); the durability proof already showed it is insufficient on its own, because Git does not fsync directories. Failed E1 does not license two authorities “until then.” B itself says failed E1 reopens A.

**C2.** Operation records are **evidence, never lock authority**. Each mutation and its recovery identify the existing serialization resource and pass its live opaque scope/handle. No operation-directory creation, PID, TTL or journal generation constitutes ownership. Same-id requests have one immutable request digest and one transition writer under that context. Concurrent identical request returns busy or the established receipt; different request is refused. The same id cannot be used on two disjoint serialization resources (P-WAL-ID).

**The one-transition-writer primitive (P-WAL-ID fold).** The model check could not test P-WAL-ID because no primitive was named; without one, its negative control reached two transition writers in two steps, and the Refs row of the R30 table takes no registry lock. The primitive is the **no-overwrite install of the next numbered record**; it is evidence ordering, not lock authority (C2 above is unchanged):

1. Id binding: record 0 (Intent, carrying request digest and canonical resource set) is installed no-overwrite. A requester that loses the install reads the installed record 0: same digest returns busy or the established receipt; a different digest or resource set is refused (`operation-id-conflict`). That is also the cross-resource rule.
2. Transition writer: the writer of transition *n* is the one whose record *n* install succeeded. A writer that loses the install of record *n* stops before any effect of that transition, re-reads the chain and returns busy, or continues only as a recovery reader.
3. Effects: only the installer of the immediately preceding record, or a recoverer that holds the operation's own resource authority (§5) and has installed its own next record, performs the next effect. Where an operation has no registry lock (Publication pins and CAS, Remote), its effect must itself be an exact-old CAS. Exact-old CAS alone does **not** make a repeat harmless: after an ABA a second CAS from the same expected value wins again (the M1 and P-WAL-ID re-checks both reached two publications for one operation this way). Rule 4 therefore applies to every effect, with or without a registry lock. An operation kind with neither a registry lock nor an exact-old effect is not exposed through an operation id until it has one. RepositoryConfig.ensureFetchMapping now uses repo-config 80 and native config.lock.
4. **TargetAttempt: no blind re-effect.** Before an irreversible effect (the target CAS of a Land or PublicationCas, a ref delete, a worktree destroy or move, a restore), its performer installs a durable `TargetAttempt` record carrying `priorTargetAttempt: none` and the expected and next values; the effect runs only after that install succeeded. Its outcome (observed success, observed loss with the actual tip) is the next record. A chain that holds a TargetAttempt without an outcome means the outcome is unknown: no recoverer reissues that effect under this operation id, whatever it observes; for a CAS, `ref == expected` is not evidence that the CAS never ran (ABA), and `ref == next` is effect observed, never attribution. It returns `recovery-required` (or completes a receipt as `effectObservedOnly` when the state reached next) and preserves WAL, pins and snapshots. A recoverer may perform the effect only by itself winning the no-overwrite install of the operation's first TargetAttempt record; a valid chain without any TargetAttempt is the WAL evidence that no prior attempt happened. A crash after the TargetAttempt install and before the effect is accepted as unknown, which keeps the effect at most once. An observed loss ends that operation; a retry against the new tip is a new operation id, because the id binds the expected values (§2), and that is how Event Store's §10 retry reaches the port. A recoverer that holds the resource authority also observes the actual effect state named by the payload's recovery observations before deciding; authority, a new transition record, equality, reflog absence, custody or a verification digest is never evidence that an effect did not happen. Clarified 2026-10-21: the Publication pin `refs/fgos/git-operations/<id>/candidate` is outside that ABA hazard. Its name is unique to one operation id, it is written exactly once before Prepared, and after the operation is terminal nothing writes it but the release delete. So for a terminal PublicationCas whose last record is `PinReleaseAttempt`, a pin still equal to the recorded candidate proves the delete did not take effect, and the recoverer re-issues the same exact-old delete under that id; an absent pin completes as `PinReleased`; any other value keeps `recovery-required` and preserves the evidence. A native `<pin>.lock` or `packed-refs.lock` left by the interrupted delete is Git crash residue (§11): it is never removed, the delete fails while it exists, and a later call completes once the operator or Git has cleared it. Doctor's `git-native-locks` row reports a retained `refs/fgos/git-operations/<id>/candidate.lock` as well as `packed-refs.lock`.

4a. **Publication supersession (amendment A1, 2026-10-18).** For a PublicationCas only, a chain that ends at a TargetAttempt without an outcome does not block a **different** operation id on the same ref: a caller holding that ref's publication authority (§5: the stream's event-seal 58, held by the Event Store of that stream's own origin, the only writer of that ref per Event Store §10's chain invariant) may start a new PublicationCas whose expected value is the tip it reads. The old operation is closed with the terminal outcome `superseded` only when all of these hold, observed under that same authority:
   1. **Later winning receipt.** A later PublicationCas of the same ref has a durable receipt whose attribution is `thisInvocationWonCas`, and that receipt's expected value equals the old operation's expected or next value.
   2. **Same authority.** The later operation's Intent carries the same repository identity, the same canonical resource set (that one ref) and the same `authorityBinding` (the same stream writer under the same event-seal) as the old one. A later operation under any other authority (another stream, a legacy-import grant, a mirror fetch, a different repository identity) never supersedes.
   3. **Old domain proven gone.** No process of the old performer can still effect its CAS: no retained native lock artifact of that ref’s backend is observed (`<ref>.lock` for `files`; the shared `tables.list.lock` for `reftable`, which a retained one blocks for every ref until the §11 exact-owned quarantine), and the old performer's process domain is proven empty by the holder-lock §8 predecessor rule, or the old performer's git children ran borrowed under the old event-seal hold (§5 `run_borrowed`) so that the later event-seal acquire through holder-lock, completing the §8/§8.1 predecessor admission, shows no live holder of it (a raw kernel acquire alone is not proof on Windows, where an orphan child can outlive the handle; the model check found a 4-step counterexample). `containment-unproven`, an unknown or unlistable domain, or a live holder keeps the old operation `recovery-required`. **Required so that condition 3 is decidable:** every git child of a PublicationCas runs borrowed under the caller’s event-seal hold (§5 `run_borrowed`), with hooks disabled (§4 C1), so a Publication git child starts no descendant outside that borrow; an event-seal acquire through holder-lock that completes the §8/§8.1 predecessor admission therefore proves the old performer’s domain gone; on Linux and macOS the borrowed description makes that admission immediate once every borrowing child has exited. A Publication route that cannot lend the event-seal to its git children refuses before Intent (`publication-borrow-unavailable`) instead of running unborrowed.

   The `superseded` record is the old chain's outcome record after its TargetAttempt, installed by the no-overwrite primitive of rules 1-2 (one transition writer: a recoverer of the old id that loses the install stops at `EEXIST`; one that wins first leaves the chain under rule 4 and the supersession attempt stops). It names the later operation id and its receipt digest. The old operation's attribution is `effectObservedOnly` when the later expected value equals the old next value, and `uncertain` otherwise; it is never `thisInvocationWonCas`. Supersession is record-only: the old CAS is never replayed, reissued or re-attempted under any id, and the old id stays bound (rule 1) and is never reused. Tip equality without the later durable receipt is never used. A superseded chain is terminal: it is not pending or unresolved for startup checks, doctor's pending count or C5 (§12), its pin may be released, and compaction treats it like any terminal chain. Every other operation kind keeps rule 4 unchanged.

4b. **Abandoned before attempt (clarified 2026-10-18 with the Git phase 01 review).** A chain that ends at Intent or Prepared has no TargetAttempt, so by rule 4 no target effect can have happened. Read-only candidate checks and quiescent admission run before Intent, and no wait separates Prepared from TargetAttempt. Under the same publication authority, with the old performer’s domain proven gone as in rule 4a condition 3, such a chain is closed record-only with the terminal outcome `abandonedBeforeAttempt` (its pin, if any, is reconciled per the recovery table) and becomes compactable; a later operation id on the same ref is never blocked by it. An operation directory that holds no installed record (empty or temporary files only) is not an Intent and is removed under the same authority. A winning PublicationCas sweeps pending same-ref chains under its authority by rules 4a and 4b before returning; a sweep that cannot decide keeps the evidence and reports it.

For Land, rule 4b applies under its destination 20/admin 40/main 50/recovery 80 authority at Intent, Prepared or verification-without-TargetAttempt. Recovery permits any writer under that destination authority to record pre-attempt abandonment and retains candidate evidence until owner acknowledgement; writer equality remains required for post-attempt recovery and cleanup. It never repeats merge or CAS.

For ScopedCommit and Sync, rule 4b also applies under their verified checkout custody: no ref attempt exists at Intent/Prepared; a recorded outcome, released Sync pin and the same WAL compaction close the chain without replay.

4c. **Config approved (amended 2026-10-18 with the Event Store phase 05 review).** When every requested mapping already exists, ensureFetchMapping re-approves the observed remote URL by recording ConfigChange Intent followed directly by the terminal outcome `configApproved`. The Intent retains URL and refspec provenance; no target effect, TargetAttempt or Transfer occurs. `configApproved` is valid only for ConfigChange before any TargetAttempt; the operation id stays bound (rule 1). fetchMappingProvenance recognizes this approval alongside successful config installation. No other rule changes.

5. Directory creation, PID, TTL and record age never decide anything in this protocol.

Versioned immutable numbered records carry canonical digest/predecessor digest; write exclusive temp with every byte written and checked (a short write or `EFBIG` leaves a partial temp, which is never a record), fsync file, atomically install **without overwrite**, fsync directory **and parent-dir durability**. Ordinary `rename` overwrites an existing target (the durability proof replaced `old-record` this way), so the install uses `renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE)`, or `link(temp, final)` followed by `unlink(temp)` with recovery treating a surviving temp name as an orphan, or the platform's audited no-replace equivalent. Newly created ancestor directories (the operation directory itself) are fsynced, and their parent, before the first record is acknowledged. Parse/hash/version/chain error is corrupt, never empty.

2026-10-19: replaying an already installed Intent syncs its operation directory and WAL operations parent before returning the existing binding, including when a previous creator stopped before syncing either directory. The intact-candidate connectivity fast path trusts history reachable from the expected published tip and walks only the candidate's new reachability, as Git's connectivity check does; failure falls back to the complete type/history checks.

**Directory durability is Git's own job.** Git 2.53.0 never fsynced a parent directory in any traced primitive, even with `core.fsync=all` (the durability proof traced `hash-object`, `commit-tree`, `update-ref --stdin` and `commit`: file fsync then rename, no directory fd). So `core.fsync` is a backend input, never evidence of `stableMedia` by itself, and `fgos-git` itself fsyncs, in this order: every loose-object fan-out directory and `objects/` it wrote into before the WAL `prepared` record and before any ref that references those objects; the directory chain of each ref it created or changed (including new `refs/fgos/...` directories, and `packed-refs`' directory) after the CAS and before the receipt; the WAL directories as above. Until E1 proves a filesystem/backend profile, the receipt advertises `durability.processCrash` only.

**ScopedCommit objects.** The blobs and trees ScopedCommit stages (`git add`, `write-tree`) must not reach their final name before their bytes are durable, and Git moves the branch inside `git commit`. Both stagers therefore run with `-c core.fsync=loose-object`: Git 2.53 then fsyncs each loose file under its temp name before it links it (traced for `add` and `write-tree`; `mktree` ignores `core.fsync`, even `all`), so a power cut leaves a temp file, never an empty object under the final name. `core.fsyncMethod=batch` is not used: it links the first object of a run after only a writeout hint, and that object is exactly the one a cut can leave empty. Git does not fsync directories and does not touch an object that already exists, so the objects are still made durable before `git commit` runs: after `write-tree`, `fgos-git` lists the staged change with one `diff-tree --raw -t -r` of the baseline tree against the written tree (it replaces the earlier staged-set read, so no Git child is added; it also gives the oid of every changed tree and blob, which the index diff does not), then fsyncs each file, its fan-out directory and `objects/` in one concurrent batch, through the one loose-object durability function Publication also uses, before the intent is written; the file fsync stays because it is the only cover of an object that already existed loose (written by plain Git, a hook or an earlier release without `core.fsync`) and of the directory entries, and it costs little on files Git just synced. Git never rewrites an existing loose file, so an object an earlier cut left empty (or not zlib) would be named by the new commit: before the batch the staged objects are checked, and a corrupt one is removed and the selection staged again (index entries of the selected paths dropped so Git hashes the worktree afresh; a tree Git cannot even list is found from the object ids Git prints), at most eight rounds, else the commit is refused with `object-file-corrupt` before `git commit`, HEAD and the index as they were. The same function refuses `object-file-corrupt` for any caller rather than syncing such a file. A branch tip therefore never names a staged blob or tree that a power cut can still lose, acknowledged or not. After `git commit`, and before the `Commit` outcome is installed (live and in recovery), a second batch makes the commit object, its fan-out directory and `objects/` durable together with the branch's directory chain; it covers every object the observed commit created (from the post-commit `show --raw -t`; recovery reads `rev-list --objects <observed> ^<baseline>`), so a hook that changed the tree is covered too, and objects already made durable are skipped. Packed objects need nothing. A cut between Git's ref move and the receipt can leave the branch naming a commit whose commit object or ref directory chain is not durable yet, and that commit has no receipt; recovery makes them durable before it records the outcome. If an object of such an unacknowledged tip is unreadable, recovery answers `recovery-required` with cause `commit-objects-unreadable`, never resets the branch and leaves the chain open as evidence. E1 (`scripts/git/e1-power-cut.sh --kind scoped`) judges the branch tip at every replay point: its history lists, every tree and blob opens and `fsck` is clean, with no excused error, and the trace rules check that each staged object's file is fsynced before it is linked to its name (R8), that its file and directories are fsynced before the ref rename and the commit's after the link and before the receipt. The fixture's halfway `pack-refs` runs with `core.fsync=all` so packed-refs is fsynced before its rename (R9); the fixture is not a judged operation.

Discriminated payload (panelist 1) — A's universal `target.next` + candidate parents cannot express delete / worktree move / config / pre-OID scoped commit:

```text
Intent {
  version, operationId, requestDigest, repoIdentity,
  kind, resourceSet, authorityBinding, writerIdentity,
  payload: RefUpdate | RefDelete | Land | ScopedCommit | Sync |
           WorktreeCreate | WorktreeMove | WorktreeRemove |
           Restore | ConfigChange | RemoteFetch | RemotePush | PublicationCas,
  previousDigest: null
}
Record {version, operationId, sequence, previousDigest, phase,
        observedFacts, effectReceipt?, error?, digest}
```

Each payload specifies preconditions, irreversible boundary, snapshot/pin needs, recovery observations, and terminal outcomes. The phase graph is operation-specific. `intent → prepared → target-attempt → ref-published → checkout-reconciled → complete` is the **Land** graph, not universal; every graph puts a durable TargetAttempt record (§10 rule 4) immediately before its irreversible effect. Publication-only skips checkout. Nested pin writes are prepare substeps, not recursively new top-level WAL operations. An object write with no published ref is not a completed publication.

Before durable prepared, unreferenced objects may disappear and must be rebuilt/reverified; they are never acknowledged publication. Read-back is mandatory; a record alone is not proof of effect.

| Actual state on recovery | Required action |
|---|---|
| ref=expected, prepared candidate intact, valid chain with no TargetAttempt record | Re-attempt only by winning the no-overwrite install of the operation's first TargetAttempt record, with valid custody/evidence/snapshot and the operation's resource authority; else preserve/refuse |
| ref=expected, TargetAttempt record without an outcome record | Outcome unknown: never reissue the CAS, even with valid custody/evidence (an ABA may hide a win). `recovery-required`; preserve WAL, pins and snapshot. Equality, reflog absence, custody, verification digest or a new transition record is not evidence that the CAS never ran. For a PublicationCas, a new operation id on the ref may proceed and rule 4a may later close this one as `superseded` |
| ref=next, publication-only | Validate objects/parents; report effect-observed; claim this-invocation only with WAL evidence |
| ref=next, checkout/index=next | Complete missing receipt; markers may lag effect |
| ref=next, checkout/index exactly captured old | Guarded finish under locks/custody then verify |
| Land ref=next, LandVerified explicitly records ignored-store collision | Record `pendingStoreCollision`, observed-only, without touching checkout/index |
| ref=next, mixed state/user edits | Preserve snapshot/current bytes; recovery-required, no blind read-tree/reset/abort |
| Land ref is a strict descendant of next, TargetAttempt without outcome | The unique candidate in its ancestry proves the effect landed. Record `Outcome::Land { checkout: "pending", observed_only: true }`; receipt `checkout: pending` with `effectObservedOnly` means the checkout may hold an unreconciled or reverted state. Never touch checkout/index or reconcile against this later tip. Exact next still requires the guarded recovery rows above. |
| ref neither expected nor next | Never roll back another writer; ancestry/reflog as evidence only; retain pins; report supersession/uncertainty; for a PublicationCas, rule 4a |
| PublicationCas: TargetAttempt without outcome, and a later PublicationCas of the same ref with a durable `thisInvocationWonCas` receipt (any actual ref value) | Close as `superseded` only when every condition of §10 rule 4a holds (later receipt with expected = old expected or old next, same authority, old domain proven gone, no retained `<ref>.lock`); install the outcome record by no-overwrite; never replay, reissue or re-attempt the old CAS; attribution `effectObservedOnly` or `uncertain`, never this-invocation; a failed condition keeps `recovery-required` with WAL, pins and snapshot preserved |
| target=next, pin=expected and/or a retained `<ref>.lock` (residue of a multi-ref transaction written by a legacy writer or raw Git, an external pin rewind, or tamper; Git's own pin-first order cannot produce it, since a death between its pin and target steps leaves pin=next, target=expected) | Read both refs back; reconcile only from the WAL record: CAS the pin forward to the recorded candidate, then read it back, only when a valid prepared record bound to this operation id, request digest, resource set and candidate exists and the target is at next; a chain that ends at Intent, or is corrupt, is preserved and fails closed, never promoted to prepared because the target equals next; never replay the target transaction, never roll the target back, attribution stays `effectObservedOnly` unless the WAL holds this invocation's evidence. A retained native ref lock is handled by §11 (exact owned dead-child quarantine with inode recheck), never by age or PID unlink |
| read-tree interrupted: worktree files already at next, index still old, `index.lock` present | Do not retry because the target ref matches: the retry failed with `Entry not uptodate` in the floor proof. Preserve the exact lock and current files; complete only when recorded per-path pre/post fingerprints and the owned index evidence prove the state, otherwise `recovery-required` |
| corrupt journal/missing required object | Fail closed with evidence location; never reconstruct intent by guess |

Child death/timeout around CAS is unknown until read-back. Startup mutation checks pending entries touching its resources; **bounded scan returning incomplete knowledge is not clearance to mutate** (C4). A pending entry blocks a new mutation of the same resource and always blocks any replay or re-attempt of its own operation id, with two narrow admission exceptions: a pending PublicationCas does not block a different operation id on the same ref that holds the same publication authority; §10 rule 4a resolves the pending one; a new Land id holding the same destination slot may prepare while old Land WAL/pins remain protected only if the old chain has no TargetAttempt or the current destination is neither its next tip nor a descendant of it. Otherwise admission returns `resource-recovery-required` naming the old id until recovery completes. The latter exception applies only to the destination resource, never the old candidate checkout/lifecycle resources, and it never bypasses the real clean/native-state gate or authorizes replay of the old id. Thus unresolved evidence does not become a permanent destination WAL block; unresolved native locks or user edits still require their named safe repair. Doctor is read-only. Explicit repair/supervisor uses the same crate/locks; no separate Node repair core.

Unresolved records/pins/snapshots never expire by TTL. Doctor reports pending count, retained bytes, oldest unresolved operation and consumer acknowledgement debt. New preparation may refuse with a named storage-capacity cause; it must **never** recover capacity by deleting unknown evidence. Compaction retains request digest and durable terminal receipt needed to reject same-id reuse; a `superseded` chain (rule 4a) is terminal and compacts the same way, keeping its request digest and its `superseded` outcome with the superseding operation id; consumer ack does not make the id reusable. WAL compaction never authorizes event segment deletion. Clarified 2026-10-19: compacted PublicationCas entries retain expected and next along with the request/binding digests and terminal outcome, so `recover(id)` returns the same full terminal receipt (or terminal error with that receipt) under the original publication authority, without replaying a CAS. The version-2 compact ledger uses lossless base64url encoding of hex hashes to keep the 512-byte per-entry bound, including SHA-256 and maximum-length ids. Version-3 Sync entries additionally retain the recorded symbolic reference in the digest; version-1/2 receipts remain readable. Compaction remains per call. A scan that encounters a disappearing or partial operation chain re-reads its durable ledger entry: a validated ledger proves it terminal and its bytes are counted in the ledger pass; absence or corruption still fails closed. Admission reports deferred compaction errors on stderr and retains the evidence. WAL scans skip `.durable-file-*.tmp` before counting their bound, including the compacted ledger, using the same temporary-name rule as operation readers; installed malformed entries still fail closed. Publication close consumes holder-lock release and returns `{released, leftoversKilled}`, or `lock-release-stuck` with killed count and pids; dropped handles use the same settle (§5 of Holder-lock).

**Journal implementation facts (2026-10-23).**
- **Filesystem and directory syncs.** The WAL runs on the `fgos_durable_file::FileSystem` trait, and its directory fsyncs go through that crate's `sync_directory` and `sync_directories`. The Git crate keeps no filesystem or directory-sync copy of its own.
- **`core.fsyncMethod`.** Every Git child that runs with `core.fsync=all` is built by `durable_args` (`packages/git/rust/src/lib.rs`), which also forces `core.fsyncMethod=fsync`. A repository or global `writeout-only` can therefore no longer turn a reported durable effect into one that a power cut rolls back.
- **Digest.** The record digest keeps the crate's own canonical encoder (`wal::canonical_digest`: integers exact, keys in byte order, floats refused). It does not use the shared canonical-JSON leaf, which prints numbers through f64 and orders keys by UTF-16: the two encoders cannot agree for every future record, and a digest change is a WAL-version event.
- **Compaction.** Land, ScopedCommit, Sync, SafeRestore (on acknowledgement) and Publication chains compact as described above. **Worktree and Refs chains are not compacted**: they stay retained, and ConfigChange chains stay retained because `hooks_provenance` reads them. Refs, Worktree and RepositoryConfig admission read the WAL once through `wal.active` (Refs and Worktree share `refs::admission`), which fails closed on a corrupt or truncated chain, a stray entry, a pre-intent artifact and the bound, as Publication's admission does; the Worktree prune and the doctor inspection still scan. A retained chain costs one `inspect` per admission, so admission stays O(retained chains); doctor's `git-operation-journal` row prints the retained-chain count, bytes and oldest age (a warning past 64 chains or 7 days), and they are the signal to schedule compaction. Compaction off the hot path (through a doctor fix or the guarded prune door, never on every add) is a backlog item (`docs/backlog.md`, p-djrgdijd2c); its sentence in this section comes before its code.

**C4 ENOSPC/fsync.** ENOSPC, inode exhaustion, permission failure and failed fsync **before** the effect refuse; **after** the effect return observed/pending durability, not ordinary no-effect failure. Injected pre-effect faults behaved this way on the floor (write ENOSPC on a loose object and fsync EIO on a ref lock both exited 128 with no ref change); a failure writing the receipt after an effect keeps pending durability and all existing evidence. Post-effect injection is still unproven. Must not report durable success without advertised persistence of objects, ref **and** journal. Process-crash read-back is not power-loss durability: stable-media claims require measured fsync/filesystem support, reported by doctor. Failed support narrows stated capability or reopens design.

## 11. Filesystem repair and safe cleanup

Native index.lock/ref.lock/packed-refs locks are not Holder-lock files. Existence/age/PID guess never suffices to unlink. Uncertain live ownership yields busy/recovery-required. Quarantine only exact owned dead-child artifact under corresponding exclusion with operation identity and immediate inode/state recheck; preserve contents/provenance. Unknown external lock requires inspected repair, never force. Do not write Linux `/proc` as the portable ownership test (`holders()` is `unlistable` on macOS/Windows).

Under worktree-admin 40 (holder-lock §16 as amended for Q6), in one hold validate worktree canonical identity, not main/root/live cwd, custody, staged/unstaged/untracked state, pending operation, delivery/content proof and current tip. Dirty/uncertain trees are preserved/relocated with warning; remove failure never falls through to rm-rf (supersedes G-046 `finally` force-remove and live `integrate.mjs` `fs.rmSync` fallback). Missing directory alone does not justify prune: inspect registration/backlinks/ownership. The Worktree prune request may name an absent path: under admin 40 it proves a successful create WAL for that exact path/registration, verifies the common-dir backlink and directory identity, then records the removal and its ownership digest before deleting only known native registration files. Unknown ownership, locks, extra residue or a detached tip lacking a delivery ref preserve the registration with a named refusal. Interrupted create/relocate journals paths/registration and reconciles before consumer session registry settlement. Recovery classifies at least four residue shapes the floor proof produced by killing Git at fixed barriers: an interrupted create left `locked initializing` (`worktree repair` does not unlock it; unlock only with operation-owned evidence, never `move -f -f`); a move killed after the directory rename (bytes at the destination, backlink at the source, listed `prunable`; repair at the WAL's recorded destination, never a searched-for path); a removal complete with admin entry pending; and a partly pruned admin entry (`worktree list` omission is not proof that admin artifacts are gone). Unlock and prune run under worktree-admin 40 with operation-owned evidence from the WAL.

SafeRestore checks full locked status, target commit and ancestry then creates durable binary/untracked/mode-preserving snapshot before destructive effect. No retry reset/clean discards producer writes. Cleanup requires consumer harness/retrospective gate separate from land; patch-id fallback is reported as limited evidence. Unknown proof preserves branch. Delete is expected-tip CAS under worktree-admin 40, with every registered checkout revalidated in the same hold (§3), and refuses an in-use branch. Cleanup warning never negates completed land. G-193: preserve valuable bytes and session identity; legacy force is not a data-loss bypass. Forced clean detached-session cleanup uses SafeRestore under the same custody to retain the prior HEAD at `refs/fgos/git-operations/<operationId>/candidate` with an unacknowledged snapshot, then removes the checkout at its recorded start commit. The warning names that retained ref and snapshot; dirty or uncertain checkouts keep their session registration and store link. SafeRestore records detached identity as `HEAD`, revalidates attached/detached identity before effect and during recovery, and never releases the retained pin on session removal.

2026-10-21 (phase 07 unit 24): every checkout the runner provisions (a dispatch or claim worktree from `createWorktree`, a verify checkout from `createVerifyCheckout`, used by `fgos return` and by approve's already-merged verification) is provisioned through `provisionWorktree`, which records what the dependency install and `worktreeSetup.commands` placed under the state root. `removeWorktree`, like the `fgos worktree remove` verb, removes through `removeProvisioned`: the recorded bytes are set aside before the Worktree port judges the checkout, put back when the port refuses, and deleted with the record once the checkout is gone. Bytes the record does not name still make the port refuse.

2026-10-20 (phase 06 unit 4): Rust `preview_restore`/`apply_restore`/`recover_restore`, addon `GitSafeRestore` and Node `createGitSafeRestore` expose `preview({request}, held?)`, `apply({operationId, request}, held?)` and `recover({operationId}, held?)`. Request binds full `expectedHead`, descendant `target`, nonempty caller `reason` and optional `reapplyLocalDelta`. Preview performs full status/commit/ancestry/native-state checks under the existing checkout door without writing Git/index/WAL. Linked producers require the exact verified custody lease and acquire no additional resource lock; main uses 50. Config facts remain operation-local and stamp-validated before every child.

Restore reuses ScopedCommit's `SavedPath` capture with no-follow bounded reads and full permissions. The protected set includes incoming leaves, every ancestor, and all existing descendants of directories replaced by incoming leaves. **With or without `reapplyLocalDelta`, untracked and ignored collisions refuse by default** (`restore-untracked-target-collision`). Only exact paths explicitly listed in `allowedReplacements` may be replaced; directory permission is not recursive permission. Their bytes are captured before reset. Unrelated ignored paths and runtime `.fgos` remain untouched; symlink parents refuse.

The digest-verified snapshot at `<common-dir>/fgos/git/restore-snapshots/<digest>` holds dirty/staged, untracked, ignored-collision and absent paths, raw index, staged/unstaged binary deltas and modes. Clean tracked bytes remain in Git objects; only their permission metadata is saved. The 32 MiB aggregate captured-file/index budget and 128 MiB serialized ceiling therefore do not charge clean tracked content. Unsupported index flags, submodules and special files refuse; intent-to-add is supported and restored after reapplication. The WAL remains `intent → prepared → target-attempt → restore-applied → outcome`, followed by `restore-acknowledged` only on explicit acknowledgement. Each snapshot binds its operation id, so corrupt evidence cannot prevent a new operation with identical local bytes. A completed reset/reapply with a failed post-check records `restored` plus `restore-post-check-failed`, rather than claiming the restore failed.

Reapplication preserves staged/worktree separation. To refuse offset/fuzz guesses, any locally modified path whose content also changed at the destination refuses before the attempt with `restore-overlapping-content` (under `restore-reapply-required`). Incoming executable-bit changes survive local content edits, retaining local non-executable permission bits; a competing local executable-bit change refuses with `restore-local-mode-conflict`. Reapply failure after the attempt retains the original snapshot and requires observation. Recovery never repeats reset or reapply. Pre-attempt chains close abandoned; exact recorded post-state may close `effectObservedOnly`; mixed/old/third-party state closes `recoveryRequired` with evidence retained. An unreadable original or post snapshot closes the active chain with its exact `restore-snapshot-unreadable` cause and `closed-with-evidence-retained`; it leaves damaged files untouched and permits a fresh fully checked operation. Native lock residue remains a named refusal.

Retention is **caller-acknowledgement bounded**: callers must recover/extract needed bytes and call `acknowledge({operationId}, held?)` on terminal operations. Before acknowledgement, snapshot bytes and full WAL remain available without a time limit, including uncertain/corrupt evidence. `listSnapshot({operationId}, held?)` lists original snapshot entries (clean paths are not listed; read them from `expectedHead`); `extractPath({operationId, path, destination}, held?)` writes one retained file's bytes, or a symlink's literal target bytes, into a caller-given existing directory outside the repository. It uses the original basename, refuses overwrite, and creates regular files with private permissions. Absent entries have no extractable bytes. Both use the existing Restore authority/custody checks. Acknowledgement durably records `restore-acknowledged`, releases the operation's original/post snapshots only when no unacknowledged retained chain references them, then compacts through the normal terminal receipt ledger. An interrupted byte cleanup can be retried while its complete WAL binding is still present. On snapshot storage, valid unreferenced digest snapshots and recognized durable-write temporary files older than seven days are removed (the orphan retention rule); referenced, corrupt and unknown artifacts are never aged out or recursively deleted. After acknowledgement, extraction/replay requires a new operation id; acknowledgement authorizes releasing bytes. Callers must not acknowledge valuable evidence before extraction. Production migration remains phase 07; the general Recovery port is implemented for ConfigChange through repo-config authority; the twelve-kind pre-attempt interruption matrix below is covered, while production index residue remains inspection-only; cooperative termination acceptance is recorded in the unit-12 report. Impact-analysis: inactive on the implementation machine.


2026-10-20 (phase 07 unit 21): SafeRestore gains an **operator-confirmed main-checkout discard**; `fgos main-checkout-reset` is its caller. (1) `RestoreRequest.confirmedBy` is an optional non-empty string naming the confirming surface and writer identity; it is omitted from the serialized request when absent, so existing request digests are unchanged. (2) A discard on the main checkout is allowed only with `confirmedBy` (otherwise `restore-discard-requires-producer` stands); `confirmedBy` on any other request, including a linked-producer discard, refuses `restore-confirmation-unexpected`. Main 50 and the existing discard pin of the old HEAD plus the snapshot of the local delta apply unchanged; no second capture path exists. (3) For that request only, tracked `.fgos` paths are data paths (captured in the snapshot, collision-checked, reset) instead of `restore-protected-fgos-path`; untracked or ignored `.fgos` runtime state stays untouched, and untracked files elsewhere stay in place as under `reset --hard` while their bytes remain in the snapshot. Every other request keeps the `.fgos` rules. (4) The target may be any commit, sideways or backward, because the pin keeps the old HEAD reachable; unconfirmed requests keep the descendant rule. (5) A stopped merge, cherry-pick or revert is tolerated: MERGE_HEAD / CHERRY_PICK_HEAD / REVERT_HEAD residue and unmerged index entries (`ls-files -v` tag `M`, stage != 0) no longer refuse. The bytes of MERGE_HEAD, MERGE_MSG, MERGE_MODE, AUTO_MERGE, CHERRY_PICK_HEAD and REVERT_HEAD, when present, go into the snapshot's `nativeState` map (omitted when empty, so old snapshots parse and digest unchanged) and `reset --hard` clears them. `index.lock`, `HEAD.lock`, `rebase-merge`, `rebase-apply`, `sequencer` and submodules still refuse.

2026-10-20 (phase 07 unit 22): an unconfirmed forward main-checkout restore (descendant target, no `confirmedBy`, no `discardLocalDelta`) treats tracked `.fgos` paths as data paths while every one of them is clean. Clean means Git, through the driver, sees no difference from HEAD for any tracked `.fgos` path: `diff` (index against work tree) and `diff --cached --ita-visible-in-index HEAD` over `.fgos` are both empty, so a staged or unstaged edit, a deletion and a type change all count as dirt; no mtime or size shortcut decides it. When clean, the tracked `.fgos` paths of the index and of the target are collision-checked, captured in the snapshot as any data path (a clean path keeps only its mode, as other clean paths do) and updated to the target by the reset; a target that adds, removes or changes `.fgos` paths is therefore fine. A target path that would add a tracked `.fgos` file over an untracked or ignored file refuses by the existing `restore-untracked-target-collision` rule; untracked and ignored `.fgos` runtime state is otherwise never read or touched. When any tracked `.fgos` path differs from HEAD the restore refuses `restore-protected-fgos-path` and changes nothing. Confirmed discards (unit 21) and linked-producer restores keep their rules unchanged: a linked checkout's `.fgos` stays preserved worker state. This is the same data-path machinery the confirmed discard uses, selected by cleanliness instead of confirmation.
