Native `index.lock` residue is **inspection-only** and the inspection is Rust-only: `repair_native_index_lock` (`packages/git/rust/src/native_lock.rs`) runs under the existing checkout authority (main 50 or exact linked producer custody) and never moves unowned bytes. `NativeLockReport.inspection` includes visible open-descriptor holders by device/inode (pid, executable, start time), size, mtime/age, optional `index~pid.lock` diagnostic text, and the verbatim retained-lock runbook below. Hidden/unreadable processes and racing descriptors mean an empty snapshot never proves abandonment. Sidecar pid text, pathname, age and process identity do not authorize adoption. The refusal `native-index-lock-unowned` points at the runbook; no Node door reaches the inspection (the former `createGitNativeLockRepair` and its addon class were retired 2026-10-23). The result codes `created`, `landed`, `conflicted-open`, `conflict-aborted`, `cancelled` and `published-checkout-pending` were removed from §2 the same day: no port returned them; Sync keeps `conflicted-open` and `conflict-aborted` as its own state values.

fgOS prevents its own deadline/output-error residue by SIGTERM to the borrowing child's group, a bounded three-second grace, then SIGKILL and the unchanged borrow drain/reach rule. Deadline refusal reports `terminated` or `killed-after-grace`. The direct Git containment path also uses a three-second grace. `LocalMutation` currently serves fetch's update-ref CAS (not the index); checkout/index mutations use the borrowed path. No extra Git execution is added to cleanup.

The shared exact-owned quarantine path remains production for ConfigChange staging files created by fgOS with exclusive creation. The crate-private `#[cfg(test)]` index recorder remains only a fixture for that shared path; no production index producer records ownership at spawn time. It returns `absent`, `quarantined`, `ownerLiveOrUnknown` or `inspectedRepairRequired` and preserves unknown, unreadable, substituted and live artifacts. Land, SafeRestore, ScopedCommit and Sync refuse with `native-index-lock-unowned`, naming this §11 runbook. Recovery remains observe-only; a new operation uses a fresh id.

Frozen fd-table attribution at fgOS's own kill door is deferred. Its explicit trigger is an observed **`killed-after-grace` with retained `index.lock` residue**. Only then consider freeze → observe exact writable descriptor inode → kill → post-death state verification. Spawn-time pid/start-time/absence evidence is not inode ownership and is not planned.

`createGitRepositoryConfig(...)` provides `inspectHooks({ownedPath})`, `ensureHooks({ownedPath, operationId})`, `removeOwnedHooksConfig({ownedPath, operationId})` and `recover({operationId})`. Hooks paths must be absolute. A hooks slot is empty only when no file-backed config scope supplies core.hooksPath. Global/system/included values are preserved and reported as `skippedExisting` with missing fgOS enforcement. Ownership and exact-old removal checks require the exact repository-owned local config origin (`<common>/config`, or the repository’s own `config.worktree` when used), read with `--show-scope --show-origin`; included files remain user-owned even when Git labels their scope local. Read-only hooks inspection reports includes without granting checkout or transport trust; the existing mutation admission can still refuse `repository-config-untrusted: include.path` when removing a local value alongside an include. Removal reveals any inherited value. Custom/pre-existing unproven values return `skippedExisting`; inspection reports the configured path, successful WAL provenance and missing executable pre-commit enforcement. Ensure fills an empty slot; removal requires both successful owned provenance and the exact current owned value. Both use the **same config writer as ensureFetchMapping**, now admitted through LockScope repo-config 80: native `config.lock`, a private draft, full old-file plus stamp-validated exact-old-value checks immediately before replacement, WAL `ConfigChange`/Prepared/TargetAttempt/Outcome and read-back. The driver's command-line safety hooks override is excluded from stored hooks facts. Effect-free owned re-approval may use `configApproved` under §10 rule 4c. Optional hooks/staging evidence preserves historical ConfigChange digests. The writer records its exclusively-created staging inode and process tuple; after writer death the shared quarantine code preserves that exact staging artifact under repo-config 80. Recovery abandons pre-attempt changes or observes post-attempt values without replaying an effect. ConfigChange is available through the general Recovery port under repo-config 80 without the recovery leaf; production setup/doctor caller migration remains phase 07. Impact-analysis: inactive (no provider registered).


Phase 06 unit 13 review facts: SafeRestore extraction skips non-existent/prunable
worktree registrations, while every existing canonical registered path remains
excluded. Terminal ref recovery releases the candidate pin before compaction.
Compacted inspection reports a kind only when the receipt unambiguously retains
it (Restore, Land, RemoteTransfer, ConfigChange); generic receipts cannot recover
the original payload kind from a request digest.

Native-lock quarantine on Linux uses `renameat2(RENAME_EXCHANGE)` with a private
directory guard. A substituted inode is exchanged back while that guard still
excludes ordinary Git lock creation. If the kernel/filesystem does not support
exchange, quarantine retains the non-overwriting hard-link fallback: a foreign
lock created during restoration is preserved and the substituted inode remains
in quarantine, explicitly reported. Before exchange, a synced `guard-<device>-<inode>` marker in the operation’s quarantine directory records the empty guard identity. After an interruption, repair itself removes only that exact empty native-path guard when the destination retains the recorded owned artifact inode, completing the move. It also retries an interruption before exchange. Replaced or nonempty guards remain preserved and reported; age never authorizes removal.

## 12. Distribution, setup and doctor

Activated release ships compatible host, **one** Git-capable Node load, Rust core, contract/registry assets and Node facade as one unit. Resolve only through activation/manifest component roots/entries, never PATH/cwd/local build. Exact new manifest schema/key belongs to distribution's existing registration contract and must be added there before packaging. No legacy Node writer fallback. Missing/wrong addon ABI/platform/schema/digest fails before mutation.

**C5.** Runtime WAL-open requires the valid controller activation tombstone (`git-fence-missing` before records). Same-digest init/upgrade runs admission to repair an old-controller activation. Linux releases alone advertise git-wal-v1 until Windows C5 scan/fence admission exists; Windows activation without that claim continues, and lock-taking Git ports refuse by name (`git-lockscope-unavailable-windows`, Q6).

C5 predecessor enumeration is scoped to the activated workspace and its linked worktrees: cwd or resolved `--dir`/`--worktree`/`--cwd`/`-C` arguments establish membership. A machine-wide fgOS payload name or pinned state root alone does not establish membership. Unknown/unreadable candidates attributed to the workspace still fail closed; a hidden host PID namespace remains unavailable. The stop-everything cutover scan retains its broader machine-wide behavior.

Activation/rollback refuses when a live predecessor writer, pending unsupported operation format or missing fence makes safe coexistence unprovable. Swapping the manifest alone does not revoke live descriptions. Rollback means activating a compatible prior release after reconciliation or refusing it with the incompatible operation/version named. It never means retrying through the legacy Node writer, erasing WAL, or resetting a published ref.

**Who runs C5 (P-RELEASE fold).** The activation door (`fgctl` init/upgrade/rollback), never the target release, runs the C5 check, through a read contract registered in distribution's activation contract: a release whose manifest lacks the Git-WAL capability may be activated only when the common-dir WAL holds no unresolved record. A `superseded` record (§10 rule 4a) is terminal, not pending or unresolved, and does not block activation or rollback. When the first release with this WAL format is activated, it places a tombstone-style fence that an older `fgctl` meets and fails on, so an old door cannot skip the check. A release whose Git writers hold no lock (today: the Land/worktree/session writers that remove, move or prune without 40, and the integrate path that merges into main without 20 or 50) counts as a live predecessor writer while any of its processes runs, so coexistence is unprovable; activating the release that swaps Land or Worktree therefore uses the holder-lock §12 stop-everything cutover with its quiesce scan. Shared modules (`merge.mjs`, `worktree.mjs`) make that swap one change for the whole merge/worktree cluster, not a per-family switch.

Owner-approved early exception (plan Q2, 2026-10-18): public Worktree create ships after phase 03; it touches no event files. Existing consumer remove/move/prune paths still switch in phase 07.

Publication-before-Land is a **capability split** (different mutation layers), never two writers for one Land effect. A release may provide Publication while checkout callers remain on current implementation; once replacement Land is exposed to a caller family, that family's old mutation path is removed in **that** release. At final component completion the whole-product mutation allowlist is zero.

**Ordering with the Event Store cutover (P-CUTOVER fold), amended by owner 2026-10-20 11:34 (Asia/Saigon).** Foundation layers first, Work later: Git caller migration no longer waits for the Event Store Work cutover. Work retains tracked `.fgos/events.jsonl`, writer shards and `merge=union`. A clearly named temporary Work-side adapter routes checkpoint/truncation-guard and compaction Git duties through ScopedCommit / Checkpoint with declared paths; the Land event sweep becomes consumer-declared candidate paths/bytes, and post-CAS event changes use a separate declared ScopedCommit / Checkpoint operation. Replacement Git ports never implicitly discover or sweep events. Work owns event policy outside Git locks; no raw Git exception survives. The WORK lane deletes the adapter and legacy event duties together with its Event Store cutover. Q4's one-release invariant and phase-08 proof gate remain.

**Main-checkout cutover runbook: retained `index.lock`.** Never remove a holder-lock mutex/lease file. The inspection report prints these manual steps verbatim:

1. Confirm no process uses the index: the report lists visible holders; additionally
   `fuser -v <git-dir>/index.lock` or `ls -l /proc/*/fd 2>/dev/null | grep index.lock`.
   If any live Git/IDE process holds it, stop; wait or close that tool. Keep the fgOS hold
   (main 50 / worktree custody 30) so no fgOS writer starts meanwhile.
2. Resolve the real path: `git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-path index.lock`.
3. Move, never delete: `mv <that path> <git-dir>/fgos/git/native-lock-quarantine/manual-<UTC ts>/`
   (or an operator directory outside the gitdir). Record the move in the operation's evidence.
4. Re-run observe-only recovery for the operation id named in the refusal
   (`fgos ... recover <operationId>`), inspect the recorded effect, then start a fresh
   operation id. Never repeat the old attempt.
5. If the content of the moved file matters (it is Git's partially written next index), keep
   it until the operation is acknowledged; `index.lock` bytes are never needed to rebuild a
   worktree whose real `index` and `HEAD` are intact.

**Checkout recovery runbook: reflog-only candidate.** Keep the affected checkout under main-checkout 50 or linked-checkout custody, stop its writers and Git/hook descendants, and inspect the candidate OID named by recovery (`git show <oid>`) against the attempt's baseline, paths and message. If it is not this attempt's effect, preserve the evidence and delete only that inspected reflog entry under custody (`git reflog delete <recorded-ref>@{<entry>}` after checking its exact OID); rerun observe-only recovery, which records `commit-effect-unknown` (Sync: `sync-commit-effect-unknown`) when the baseline/index/quiescence conditions hold and no other candidate remains. If it is this attempt's effect, record the effect manually with its OID in the operator/consumer evidence and reconcile the checkout deliberately; do not delete that evidence to force a rejection or repeat the attempt. Until attribution is resolved the chain remains open and blocks checkout writes and Remove/Move.

2026-10-19 (landing repair): the Git driver creates its empty-hooks and temporary probe directories with explicit safe modes regardless of a group-writable umask. The registered `git-driver-directories` setup/doctor fix creates the designated fgOS state root with mode 0700, removes group/world write bits from an existing current-user-owned root only if its canonical basename is `fgos` or it carries the driver's own regular `git-floor-verdict.json` marker (a floor record with binary key and probe array), and gives its empty `git-empty-hooks` directory mode 0500. The driver and fix canonicalize a symlinked state root before checking the canonical directory. Hooks are opened with a no-follow directory descriptor before reading; emptiness, ownership and chmod use that same directory inode. The fix never chmods parents, symlinked hooks, other owners' directories or populated hooks. An unrelated unsafe root is reported and refused with instructions to correct the state-root override or permissions manually. Populated/unreadable hooks diagnostics name the path and ask the owner to inspect it, remove foreign files or restore read/search access, then rerun the registered fix. WAL/refspec setup runs this preparation first. All six Git doctor rows name the rejected directory, mode/owner, writable-directory cause and `fgos doctor --fix` remedy; read-only doctor does not repair permissions. The directory repair adds no config default or environment switch.

Setup/init registers every default/path/dependency/config in existing config-merge/check registry, respects project-over-global, installs explicit fetch mirrors and no default push config, validates common-dir WAL writability and owned hooks. Read-only checks never fix. Use dev host for edited code, not activated plain `fgos`. Implementation adds changelog entry. No production env guard-bypass for tests.

| Doctor check | Evidence |
|---|---|
| git-component-compatible | Activated host/addon/contracts/digests/Git features; **one** Git load; with a repository it also prints the trusted common-dir layout and the measured process/power-loss/fsync guarantee as information (these never fail on their own) |
| git-repository-config | Repository transport settings Publication and Remote refuse; the Event Store mirror fetch mappings (missing, obsolete exact legacy, reserved legacy mirrors); no own overwrite or default push change |
| git-operation-journal | Chain/version/readability, pending intent, pins vs actual ref; read-only; backlog/bytes/oldest unresolved under `detail`; the retained finished chains (count, bytes, oldest age; `retainedChains`, `oldestRetainedAtMs`, `ledgerEntries`, `ledgerCapacityWarning` under `detail.journal`) appear as an `info:` line, and as a `warning:` (never a failure) past 64 chains, past 7 days for the oldest, or when the id ledger is at capacity (the two limits are constants in `src/setup/git-retention-note.mjs`, no config key); fails `git-fence-missing` when a WAL exists without the C5 controller activation tombstone (the read is Inspect's `activationFence`, the same check every WAL-writing door runs); warns, never fails, about finished Land operations whose candidate worktree and pins are still retained (Recovery `listRetainedLand`: operation id, candidate path, the pins actually present, WAL age) |
| git-native-locks | Ownership uncertainty/native files; **no deletion**; points at the §11 runbook |
| git-worktree-recovery | Orphan registration, dirty/stale checkout, custody/pending operations |
| git-mutation-one-door | Zero undeclared product writers, one matcher |
| Existing holder checks | Reuse semantics/borrow-vectors/holders/fences; no duplicate primitive |

The registered fixes are four, `git-driver-directories`, `git-wal-dir`, `git-land-recovery` and `git-publication-refspecs`; a fix id is not a row id, and `registrations.mjs` owns each row's description. A failing row prints one sentence that names its next step, and the facts behind it travel in the row's optional `detail` member of `fgos doctor --json` (setup-doctor registry contract §3). One doctor run reads the repository with four `inspectGit` calls, five when `eventStore.remote` is not `origin`. Sharing a single call through the doctor-run object waits for the Work lane's `createDoctorRun`, which is not in this tree; that is the one implementation gap against the one-call target.

**Deferred: release of retained Land candidates.** `acknowledgeLand` may defer cleanup (`land-cleanup-deferred`) when the merge slot or `main-checkout` stays busy for 5 s, and a candidate also stays until Work acknowledges the result. Doctor only reports these (a warning of `git-operation-journal`; the `git-land-recovery` fix reconciles interrupted Land operations only) and states that no manual release command exists. The sweep that releases candidates whose acknowledgement Work has recorded is deferred until the Work lane resumes, because it needs Work's acknowledgement reader; doctor never guesses an acknowledgement.

Registered fixes are separate typed operations with provenance. Incomplete `git-ops-pending` scan must not clear the way to mutate.

**What `git-mutation-one-door` and V-GATE detect (source-guard fold).** A Git write is any child process whose resolved binary is `git` or a repository hook, with any argv, unless it runs through the private CLI driver of `fgos-git`. The classification proof found nine real code shapes that a scan for literal `execFile('git', ['<write verb>', …])` misses: argv injected through a callback (verb and spawn in different files), spawn through a non-`child_process` facade (`runBorrowed`), argv held in a variable or mutated after construction, global options before the verb (`-C`, `-c`, `--git-dir`, spread prefixes), wrappers that prepend the verb, a Rust `Command::new` whose binary is a variable, shell strings, non-git spawns that change Git state (direct hook replay, skill text telling an agent to run Git), and read verbs that write (`status` refreshing the index). The guard has two parts, both required: (a) a static scan of every spawn site (`child_process`, `runBorrowed`, `Command::new`, `execSync`/shell) whose binary can be `git` by data flow, including variables and wrappers, with callers of a wrapper counted; (b) a runtime check in the test suite, that records every spawn of `git` or a hook outside the driver and fails on any not on the named allowlist. Shipped skill text is scanned for instructions to run Git writes. Inspect runs with optional locks off (§3), so a read is never a write.

2026-10-20 (phase 07 unit 20, implementation): the static part is `scripts/git-one-door-scan.mjs`; the release build writes its result to `packages/git/contracts/mutation-census.v1.json` (sites, named classes and the digest of every scanned file), and `git-mutation-one-door` passes only on an empty census whose Node-payload files match the payload on disk. A binary the scan cannot resolve by data flow counts as a site, never as safe. Named classes are reported apart and never by path alone: the fgos-git driver crate; the sandbox door `scripts/lib/sandbox-git.mjs`, recognised by the `Symbol.for('fgos.git.sandbox-door')` marker on its spawn (set anywhere else, the marker is itself a site); the production sandbox probe; hook read context (read verbs in `.githooks/`); Rust test code; and, pending the owner's decision recorded in the unit 20 report, binaries taken from operator configuration or a consumer's declared verify command (`configured-binary`). The runtime part is `test/helpers/git-spawn-spy.mjs`, preloaded by `scripts/run-tests.mjs` and inherited by child processes through `NODE_OPTIONS`; it attributes a spawn to the code that called `child_process` and lets a sandbox-door spawn through only after rechecking its paths against the marked root. Scan, spy and door share one matcher (`scripts/lib/git-spawn-match.mjs`). The nine shapes above are seeded under `test/fixtures/git-one-door/` and owned by `test/git/one-door.test.mjs`.

**Owner-declared sites.** `scripts/lib/git-one-door-declarations.v1.json` is the reproducible declaration input (`git-one-door-declarations.v1`), shipped with the Node scripts and digest-bound by the census; census rows are generated, never manually moved. Each declaration names an exact relative `path`, `siteId`, supported `class`, nonempty `reason` and `retireWhen`. The supported classes are `hook-read-context`, `configured-binary` (unresolved consumer-command provenance only, never a fixed Git program), and `declared-tooling-exemption` for owner-approved evidence/tooling calls an existing typed port cannot serve, including byte-pinned reproduction tooling. The latter is reported and counted separately; it neither adds a Git port nor exempts a file or directory. These declarations implement the owner's L13-6 (1) authorization in the documentation-authority pre-flip owner record, not general permission to add raw Git callers. Duplicate, stale, missing-site or ambiguous declarations fail the scan.

A fixed-verb site's identity binds its enclosing named function and comment-blanked call text (callee, operands and options), not its line number. When the Git argv/verb is not literal at the site, including pass-through wrappers and anonymous callbacks, the identity also binds the sorted **multiset** of caller-call digests (relative caller path plus comment-blanked call text). Both named calls and function-value flows count. A new or changed resolved caller, even another read or an identical added call, makes the old declaration stale; it must be reviewed and declared again. A caller the value-flow scan cannot follow (including `.call`/`.apply`, `Reflect.apply` and array-slot calls) refuses the wrapper declaration rather than inheriting its exemption. Only `configured-binary` declarations may retain escapes: their program comes from parsed configuration, not the wrapper caller. Preceding line shifts and caller ordering alone do not change identity. The census records these `callerDigests` for review. A hook declaration's `verbs` must equal the observed literal caller verbs and all must be in the hook read set; unknown or mutating verbs, missing verbs and extra claimed verbs refuse classification even with a refreshed identity. Owning declaration proof: `test/scripts/git-one-door-declarations.test.mjs`; the single doctor wiring proof is `test/setup/git-mutation-census.test.mjs`.

2026-10-20 (owner decision Q1, recorded in phase 07 unit 21): a binary taken from operator configuration or from a consumer's declared command is the named class `configured-binary`, reported and counted, never a census site. This covers `FGOS_HOST_BIN`, `FGOS_CLAUDE_COMMAND`, `FGOS_HERDR_BIN` and other environment reads, bwrap/provider executables and other values read from parsed configuration, command-line arguments of a tool script, and a Work item's or capability's declared `verify` / invariant command run through goal-check's shell. A Git command there is the operator's or consumer's own choice inside their checkout. So a test whose declared verify command runs Git (`test/runner/merge-land.test.mjs` racing Land with `git update-ref`) is a configured-binary entry, not a spy failure. Refusing a configured binary whose basename is `git` at the spawn is a follow-up item, not part of this rule.

2026-10-20 (phase 07 unit 21): the scan resolves by data flow, never by path:
- **JS bindings.** Scope-aware bindings, including `for…of`, array and rest destructuring. Elements of arrays and objects, and of `.map`/`.find` results. Writes into an object after its literal (`out[key] = …`). The callee's parameters as the arguments of a call. Conditionals before `||`/`??`. Re-exports.
- **JS callers.** Functions stored in registries (`table[key](…)`) or as methods, and found through aliases and parameter defaults.
- **Launchers and shell scripts.** A launcher's command operand (`timeout`, `env`), and a shell script held in a variable, whose double-quoted interpolations are data operands.
- **Rust.** A renamed `Command`, and a shell's `.args(["-c", …])` script. A Rust primitive whose binary is a parameter or a field of one is the class `argv-runner`: its callers, or the struct literals of that type outside test code, are scanned instead. A constructor that passes Git is a site, an unresolved constructor stays a site, and a type built only by serde deserialisation counts as configuration.
- **New classes.**
  - `uncalled-wrapper`: a wrapper that no scanned file references outside its definition and import clauses. No product data flow reaches it; tests that call it stay under the runtime spy.
  - `production-sandbox-probe`, extended to a Git spawn whose `-C`/`current_dir` repository is proven by data flow, at every non-test caller, to lie under the run's own `temp_dir()` (holder-lock's borrow vectors).
  - Anything the fgos-git crate itself hands to a process primitive is the `git-driver` class.

2026-10-20 (owner decision, 21:06, recorded in phase 07 unit 22): a skill that is not part of the fgOS product, such as `core/skills/distill` (reference-learning clones) or `core/skills/instruction-audit` (a read-only `git check-ignore` in the user's project), sits outside the product one-door boundary. Its code is the named class `standalone-skill-script`: reported and counted, never a census site. The skill is recognised by its own declaration, `metadata.oneDoor: standalone` in its SKILL.md frontmatter, never by its path: the scan and the runtime spy both ask `standaloneSkillOf` in `scripts/lib/git-spawn-match.mjs` for the nearest SKILL.md above the spawning file. The spy now watches `core/skills/` too; a standalone skill's spawn is logged under the class, and `scripts/run-tests.mjs` counts it without failing the run. A skill without the field stays inside the boundary.

2026-10-20 (phase 07 unit 22): the scan follows these flows as well, still never by path:
- **Function values.** A wrapper that no code calls by name is followed as a value, together with the property path it sits under: aliases, arguments into resolved parameters (plain or destructured), object keys and shorthand, spreads of an object holding it, `Reflect.set` and indexed tables, `.bind(...)`, return values to their callers' call sites, and module bindings through their imports. A value passed under a key to a callee the scan cannot resolve reaches every parameter that destructures that key and every read of it. Tests (`typeof`, comparisons, `&&`) and inspecting built-ins (`JSON.stringify`, `Object.keys`, `console`) are not flows. Anything else (an array element, `.call`/`.apply`, a function passed bare to an unresolved callee) is an escape, and the wrapper keeps its site.
- **Method calls on objects.** `obj.method(args)` and `make().method(args)` return what the method of the object literal `obj` may hold returns, with the method's and the factory's parameters bound. An object literal read as a collection (`doc.tools[id]`) yields its values.
- **Rust.** The first element of a Vec a local function returns (its literal's first item, or the first `push` into the local it returns, through `split_first`) and a Vec read out of parsed data.
- **Shell.** A quoted operand of a command that never runs its operands (`echo`, `printf`, `:`, or a script function whose only use of its arguments is one of those, such as `die`) is text; a `$(...)` or backtick substitution inside it still counts, and text piped into a shell is a script.

2026-10-20 (phase 07 unit 23): with these flows the release census is empty. Still by data flow, never by path or file name:
- **Property paths.** A value keeps the exact path it is read at. `*` stands for any element (`table[key]`, `.find(..)`, `for..of`, `Object.entries`), `**` for one or more nested elements, and a numeric index (`argv[0]`) for that element of an array literal. A deep merge that reads `existing[key]` again on each level therefore stays finite. A string, a number, an object or array literal, or a function is no program: `child_process` refuses any binary that is not a string. `a && b` is `b`.
- **Configured executors (`cliSpawnAdapter`).** The executor's `invocation.command` is followed back through the dispatch stack to `resolveExecutorConfig`, then `loadRunnerConfig`, `mergeConfigDefaults` and `mergeWithGlobalConfig` (including the global config's sanitizer, a function passed as a parameter). It ends in parsed configuration files (`configured-binary`) or the literal defaults (`claude`); it never ends in `git`. No path or name decides this.
- **Functions as values, both ways.** A wrapper is resolved at every call by name **and** every place its value is called; an escape still keeps the site. A name bound to a function is a function value. A call of a parameter that holds a function (`sanitize(raw)`), or of a method on a parameter (`driver.prepare(..)`), is resolved where the callers pass the function. A value stored in a module-private `Map` or `WeakMap` (no export, no use other than its own `get`/`set`/`has`/`delete`) is any value the module `set`s. An object read out of an object a call returned keeps that call, so its parameters bind there too.
- **Callers.** A call found through a namespace import (static or `await import`), a destructured dynamic import, a re-export (`export {..} from`, `export * from`), or a spread argument (`...fn(x)`) counts. A function is referenced from another module only where that module imports it; `obj.name` is a property of something else.
- **Evaluation budget.** Within one spawn site's share, an expression already evaluated at the same place gives the same values. A cycle starts from no values and is evaluated again until its values stop growing; past four rounds it is unknown. Nothing computed from a cycle still in progress is kept.
- **Rust.** Covered forms: `if let`/`while let`/`let .. else` bindings, `match` arms with their `Some(x)` binding, `cond.then(|| v)`, a `LIST.iter().map(..).find(..)` pick from a const list, closure parameters (`opt.map(|p| ..)`), the first `it.next()`, `self.method()` locals, a `vec![first, ..]` local, tuple-struct fields (`Type(a)`, `.map(Type)`), and a module named under `use m as alias`. `containment/linux.rs` `bwrap` and `prefix[0]` resolve to the envelope's parsed `bwrapPrefix` or the standard bwrap locations.
- **N-API.** A parameter of a `#[napi(constructor)]` is also the argument at the same position of each `new <addon>.Type(..)` in the JavaScript payload, resolved there and through the JavaScript callers. `HelperExe` (Windows `semantics.rs`) resolves to the running executable, and on the Node side to the manifest's `entries.fgos` through `nativeHelperExe(addon)`. The loader now keeps that path in its own map instead of writing it onto the addon's exports object.
- **Sandbox probe through an argv runner.** An argv `[program, "-C", repo, ..]` keeps its repository with the program it resolves to. When that repository is a directory the probe creates under `temp_dir()` (also through `self` fields set by every construction), the spawn is the `production-sandbox-probe` class. This covers the holder-lock borrow vectors run through `run_borrowed` (`fgos internal lock-borrow-vectors` and the activation gate in `init.rs`). The same argv on a repository a caller names is a site.

2026-10-21 (phase 07 unit 24, review fixes): the production sandbox probe is decided only by the repository's data flow to a `temp_dir()` path; a function that merely mentions `temp_dir()` and `init` while it runs `git -C` on a caller's repository is a site. The scan lists every file of its root (inside a repository, tracked and untracked non-ignored paths through Inspect; otherwise every file on disk) and leaves out no directory by its name. It judges the product its root declares: the payload `package.json` ships (`files`, `bin`, the manifest itself), every crate the root Cargo workspace names (`members` and the separately built `exclude` crates) and the installed hooks directory; the scan counts the other files per top-level directory (`outsideProduct`) and prints that count, but the tracked census never records it, so the census changes only when the product does. A root that declares nothing (a probe tree) is judged whole. The scan also follows `promisify(<spawn function>)`, including one exported by another module, a `shell` option read through an options variable or spread (an options value it cannot read is judged both with and without a shell), a computed member of a `child_process` namespace (an unreadable key is a site), a Rust call through a module path (`pick::herdr_bin()`, or the one module file of that name in another crate) and a Rust `fn` whose generics nest. In a hook, `reflog` counts as a read only as `show` or `exists`, and `symbolic-ref` only when it names one ref and sets or deletes nothing; a shell hook line is a read only when each git command on it is such a read.

2026-10-24 (owner decision, 10:05): `.githooks/pre-commit` refuses a commit that stages a file the scan digests (same product declaration and file kinds, read through `censusDrift` in `scripts/git-one-door-scan.mjs`) whose staged digest differs from the staged census, so a push without the full suite cannot leave the census stale; it reads staged blobs only, runs no scan and builds nothing, and a commit touching no such file (or a repository without a census) is not checked. A change to the product declaration itself (`package.json` `files`/`bin`, the Cargo workspace) is left to `test/git/one-door.test.mjs` and the release build. The hook judges with the scanner of the checkout whose hook runs (resolved through `core.hooksPath`, normally main's), so a branch that changes `kindOf` or `declaredProduct` is judged by main's rules until it lands; this is the same trust choice the hook makes for `../src`.

## 13. Caller migration contract

Thin transition adapters do input/output/error translation only: no Git subprocess, matcher, lock policy, cleanup or recovery. Retain public/event output fields and timing; if format changes, version/replay changes accompany it. No dual-run mutation.

| Current production site (all mutation branches) | Replacement |
|---|---|
| `src/runner/execution/commit-unit-work.mjs` | ScopedCommit + Sync; remove add/commit/reset/abort/restore policy; its direct spawn of the `pre-commit` file goes through `git hook run` or is dropped (§7 G-062) |
| `src/runner/execution/unit-sync.mjs` | Thin Sync adapter |
| `src/runner/merge.mjs` | Land/Sync/SafeRestore/Refs/Worktree; numbering/verification policy/state remain consumer. The event sweep is the temporary Work adapter's declared Checkpoint / ScopedCommit, committed before Land pins the target; no event bytes enter a Land candidate, which for a leaf would carry `.fgos` onto a worker branch (coordinator decision, phase 07 unit 9; done in unit 12). WORK retires these legacy duties with its Event Store cutover (§12 owner amendment); no raw Git allowlist. Its decision renumbering `mv`/`add` becomes consumer-declared candidate bytes (§9 step 2). Since unit 13 the cleanup verb's branch delete is a Refs exact-old delete of the tip read before cleanup, and the main checkout's ref, its `MERGE_HEAD` and the content-integrity merge-base/merge-diff reads go through Inspect; the reads with no Inspect operation yet (`merge-tree`, `check-attr`, `ls-tree` on decisions, tree-to-tree `diff-tree` on `.fgos`, the `--ancestry-path` merge walk and the rename-detecting introduced-path diff) remain raw reads, counted in the census |
| `src/runner/worktree.mjs` | Worktree/Refs/Remote; **dependency/bootstrap (G-030/031) outside Git**. `resyncClaimWorktree` (`reset --hard`) → SafeRestore; `resyncWorktree` (verb `resync-worktree`: `reset --hard` + `apply --index`) → SafeRestore `apply` with `reapplyLocalDelta`; `refreshUnstartedBranch` (`merge --ff-only` in a checkout, else `branch -f` without CAS) → Refs `fastForwardCheckedOut` under the worktree's custody; `ensureBranchPushed` drops `push -u`, so `review` no longer writes upstream config (a user-visible change that needs a CHANGELOG line at implementation); a consumer that needs upstream config asks RepositoryConfig by name, with provenance |
| `src/workflow/integrate.mjs` | Worktree create, Land merge/squash, guarded cleanup; never Work merge import; **delete lock-free writer in the same release**. The integration target is resolved only when a Unit has a worktree or the workflow has an integrate step, through Inspect `resolveRef`, which returns unknown instead of throwing (today a detached HEAD without `origin/HEAD` throws at settle in a read-only business workflow) |
| `src/runner/repo-locks.mjs` | Reuse holder delegation; **remove** `runWorktreeAdd` spawn into Git |
| `src/runner/session.mjs` | Detached Worktree/restore/remove/prune; consumer registry atomic, no event-file workaround |
| `src/runner/loop.mjs` | Orphan delete CAS; retry reset/clean→SafeRestore; continuous custody |
| `src/setup/git-hooks.mjs` | RepositoryConfig ensure/remove-owned; preserve custom path/tracked files |
| `bin/fgos.mjs` return | Detached create/remove through admin/custody, no finally-force-delete |
| `bin/fgos.mjs` main-checkout-reset | SafeRestore preview/apply, main door and snapshot |
| `src/state/events-jsonl-truncation-guard.mjs` and `src/runner/main-fgos-checkpoint.mjs` (the spawn site of the event-file checkpoint, whose argv the guard injects through a callback; callers `claim-port.mjs` and `merge.mjs`) | Temporary Work adapter → Checkpoint / ScopedCommit with declared event paths and borrowed main authority; Work retains truncation policy; WORK deletes the adapter and event duties at Event Store cutover; no raw Git |
| `src/state/events-compaction.mjs` | Temporary Work adapter → ScopedCommit with exact old/new paths after Work verifies compaction; Work owns compaction policy and deletes this legacy duty at Event Store cutover; no replacement Git compactor |

Wave S S3 has landed on main (`bbf7b7929`, landed by `c2cadb082`, landing report `plans/reports/landing-261017-2123-main-door-state-wave-stop-everything-landing.md`); in current code every Git call of claim already runs outside the `state` transaction. Edits wait only for the second-machine landing the program board records, and for the `worktree.mjs` rows above that claim calls (`resyncClaimWorktree`, `refreshUnstartedBranch`) |
| `src/runner/github-adapter.mjs` | API merge remains GitHub; consumer safety gate; any local mutation through Git |

Frozen census §5: 46 core + 26 outer rows. This mapping covers those families plus the landed repo-lock wrapper. No old count certifies completeness: implementation must regenerate whole-product census over src/bin/packages/apps/scripts/hooks and shipped skills. Final production mutation allowlist is zero; fixture exemption must identify sandbox setup, not a production escape. Legacy line numbers are evidence, not stable identifiers. The classification proof (unit-run-1792254306706-087ee956) re-ran the census at HEAD `1e238aead` and found about 75 write lines in 17 production files, 4 scripts, 2 Rust sandbox sources and 1 skill; every site it reported as uncovered or mapped to an operation §3 lacked (its F-E1 to F-E10: checkpoint spawn site, Land event sweep and post-CAS re-stage, `resyncWorktree`, `refreshUnstartedBranch`, `push -u`, `promote-engine.mjs`, the knowledge-migration move, the skill text, the production sandbox probe, the hook-file replay) is now covered by a row above or by §3/§7. That census is evidence for this text, not the implementation census, which is regenerated with the detection rule of §12.

**Temporary Work event declaration (WORK-owned deletion at Event Store cutover).** `ScopedCommit` and `Checkpoint` accept the typed optional `legacyEvents: {exactPaths}` declaration, validated by Git only on the main checkout under main-checkout 50. Its retired layout is exactly `.fgos/events.jsonl`, `.fgos/events/<shard>.jsonl`, `.fgos/events/archive/<shard>.jsonl`, `.fgos/events/archive/<name>.manifest.json`, and `.fgos/porting/events.jsonl`; traversal, duplicate declarations and every other `.fgos` class are refused. Normal Unit scopes continue to exclude `.fgos`. Work's `src/state/legacy-event-git-adapter.mjs` declares paths and translates receipts; Work owns discovery, truncation/compaction proof and timing outside Git locks. Inspect supplies bounded literal-path last-commit timestamps for the existing fallback timing policy; object reads distinguish absent blobs from read failures. Checkpoint admits only declared tracked paths; ScopedCommit also admits declared new archive/shard paths. The exact main-only Work declaration authorizes current event bytes, including event paths already dirty at preflight; the normal producer-baseline exclusion still applies to every undeclared/non-event path. The existing hook grant-check door accepts event deletions only from this active durable ScopedCommit intent under verified main-50 borrower authority; the cutover seal grant remains separate. Land gains no implicit sweep. WORK deletes this adapter, typed declaration and temporary hook/native grant support together at its cutover.

Outer production scripts migrate too: knowledge-migration→ScopedCommit with both literal paths of the move (§3) and full content/path rollback before registry update; test-select-mutate→Worktree; install-git-hooks→RepositoryConfig. knowledge-canary, observe fixture regeneration, external-consumer CI and Rust tests may create explicitly classified sandbox repos. Hook read context is not a second writer. Generated skill surfaces are regenerated from source.

2026-10-20 (phase 07, outer scripts): an outer script reaches its own repository only through `scripts/lib/git-ports.mjs` (Inspect, ScopedCommit; it stages the checkout's addon when no host runs the script), and builds a sandbox repository only through `scripts/lib/sandbox-git.mjs`, which refuses any repository, `init` target or path argument outside the root the script created. knowledge-migration commits each move as one ScopedCommit of its two literal paths and rolls path and content back before any registry event; test-select-mutate takes its throwaway HEAD checkouts through the runner's verify-checkout door and guarded removal, so a checkout still holding run bytes is preserved and reported, never forced. Since unit 18 the rename-detecting index/worktree diffs of `test-select.mjs`, the worktree-against-commit patch of `test-proof-inventory.mjs` and the remote URL of `test-select-promote-report.mjs` read through Inspect. Two reads stay raw and counted in the census: the shell integration's common-dir read (a shell function has no door) and the test suite's config-guard read (loading the addon in every test process is a gate decision).

**Workflow deletion.** Order: pass Rust Land/Create/Cleanup conformance, switch every Workflow caller and remove lock-free implementation **in the same release**. Export compatibility facade may contain only translation; delete on last migrated import in that same change. No interim Node patch. No release exposing replacement Land may retain old lock-free writer.

**Order is the spec's; the date is the Git plan's (owner decision Q4).** The brief asked for “order and date” and also said “the spec names no phases or dates.” The owner resolved that contradiction: this spec states only the same-release invariant (switch every caller and delete the old writer in one release; whole-product mutation allowlist zero), and the Git implementation plan owns the calendar date. This spec invents no date.

S3 of wave S has landed on main (see the `claim-port.mjs` row); the earlier sentence that the board's “complete” was not landing evidence is superseded by that landing evidence. G0 does not alter 4.3-S or 4.3-R primitives. Publication is independent of claim cutover and takes no main lock.

## 14. Dissent disposition and six expertise boundaries

These are the dispositions after the owner's answers of 2026-10-17 (§18); they are decisions, not proofs.

| Topic | Accountable owner / disposition |
|---|---|
| Live Rust | Git; owner already chose A. Do not reopen. |
| Event Store protocol / 4.3-R primitives / ranks | Closed; do not reopen. Wrap set of worktree-admin closed by owner decision Q6: add, remove, relocate and prune under one rank-40 lock (holder-lock §16 amended). |
| N7 who holds custody | Execution holds, Git validates. Mechanism decided (Q2): the existing `dispatch-cwd` lease extended across the runner commit, borrowed into git children; no new entity. The lease-lifetime change is implementation work in the Git plan. |
| RUL84 | Shared foundation leaf; Execution grants, Git enforces; one Rust implementation and port. |
| Test preservation | Git maintainer preserves behaviours, one owning proof each (design principle 8); mixed internal imports may retarget, not weaken; tests of deleted mechanisms and Node copies of Rust-owned cases are retired with their owning proof named. Five lexical counts are not a classification. E6 required. |
| Workflow deletion order | Same-release switch + allowlist zero (Q4). The Git plan owns the date. |
| G-202 catchup | Frozen by owner decision Q5: isolated scoped resolver when custody exists; else abort own merge. Land aborts. |
| Node→Rust route | Decided (Q1): one combined N-API addon linking `fgos-event-store` and `fgos-git` for publication and every other Node-reachable Git operation; no host route; E3 is the reversal trigger. |
| Journal home | File WAL is the sole recovery authority (Q3); pins are reachability only; no dual write. |
| Who holds 20/40/50 | Rust `fgos-git` in its own LockScope seeded from the caller's held ranks and verified lease descriptors (Q1 option (a)); legality per the R30 table in §5. |
| Noncoding consumer | Workflow; real business-discussion inspect/evidence route. A read-only Unit needs no RepoHandle; outside a repository Inspect returns `notARepository` (§1). Whether Workflow runs without a repository is Workflow's decision (§18 open item 8). |
| Durability | Git owns common-dir WAL; Event Store owns business truth. |
| Packaging | Distribution owns manifest/activation; Git owns addon/core contract; one Node load. |
| IPC/fd | Git/Holder-lock measure batching/borrow; N-API never relaxes state exec rule. |
| Cross-runtime locking | Holder-lock rules unchanged; scope continuity through explicit held ranks and kernel-verified descriptors (option (a)), proven by M2/V-ORDER. |
| Filesystem recovery | Git reconciles without force; uncertain states preserve data. |

## 15. Verification and claims needing proof

All vectors need real temporary-repo conformance tests through Rust and the relevant Node route, deterministic barriers/fault injection rather than sleep. **T/T2/T3** are the catalogue's three frozen test inventories (T: v1 lane tests; T2: the sweep-3 redo, 2,384 tests in 109 files; T3: the sweep-4 set, 133 tests in 9 files); they are a frozen subset, not a classification. The test-preservation rule (design principle 8, owner decision 2026-10-20) is: every assertion that reaches a Git write site gets exactly one label, about the **behaviour** it proves, never about the line. **Kept**: the behaviour still exists and this assertion is its owning proof; it runs through the new port unchanged. **Retargeted**: the behaviour still exists; only the import, fixture or port under the assertion moves; the asserted outcome is not weakened. **Retired**: either the behaviour no longer exists under this spec (the mechanism is deleted or superseded — the event-file families of D-ADR0055 are one such case, not the only one), or the behaviour still exists and another named test owns it — a Rust conformance test for the port's cases, or the single Node route test of that port. A retired assertion appears in the E6 inventory with its reason and, in the second case, the `file:test` that now owns the behaviour. Retiring is not weakening; weakening is asserting less about a behaviour that still exists and is forbidden. Lines, counts, files and the frozen T/T2/T3 inventories are the population to classify, not a classification. Each retained G-id in §16 points to exactly one owning test; a G-id with two tests names the owner and retires or retargets the other. The inventory is regenerated from the current suite at implementation. At least six current test files that reach write sites are in none of T/T2/T3 (`test/runner/worktree-admin-multiprocess.test.mjs`, `test/runner/land-vs-manual-commit.test.mjs`, `test/e2e/main-checkout-borrow-hook.test.mjs`, `test/runner/repo-locks-multiprocess.test.mjs`, `test/runner/claim-capacity-race.test.mjs`, `test/holder-lock/fences-main-door.test.mjs`) and are covered by the same rule. Results of the 2026-10-18 proof runs are in §19; no V-vector has passed.

| Vector | Required behavior |
|---|---|
| V-PUB | Blob/tree/commit/CAS from linked worktree leaves HEAD/index/tree/main lock unchanged; DAG both parents; **no reference-transaction hook effect** with a hook installed; symbolic owned ref refused at preflight and at read-back; an adversarial direct-to-symbolic substitution between preflight and effect writes nothing outside the grant (containment; detection of a substitution the CAS overwrote is not required, §4 C1); replacement objects do not change reads |
| V-CAS | Competing exact-old/absent/delete updates, packed refs; one winner; death/read-back; bad objects refused; pin installed and read back before the single-ref target CAS; a kill between the pin and the target steps, and inside a multi-ref transaction written by raw Git as residue, on every supported backend, `files` and `reftable`, recovers from every observed subset through the WAL; a TargetAttempt without an outcome is never reissued, including after an ABA; a stale PublicationCas TargetAttempt (killed on either side of the target rename) followed by a new operation on the same ref under the same event-seal: the new operation proceeds, the old is closed `superseded` only after the new durable receipt, with the old domain proven gone and no retained `<ref>.lock`, and its CAS is never replayed (§10 rule 4a); a retained `<ref>.lock` is never unlinked by age or PID; CAS-delete of a checked-out branch refused by the typed port, including a branch checked out by a worktree registered after an earlier snapshot (revalidation under 40) |
| V-EVENT | Existing seal/coverage/receipt tests unchanged through new port; mirrors, divergence two parents, no-force push |
| V-GC | gc/repack/pack-refs keeps pins; interrupted unprepared objects not acknowledged |
| V-LAND | Checked-out/unattached/foreign-attached targets, dirty/index/MERGE_HEAD, ref races, real squash/merge parents |
| V-VERIFY | Evidence digest bound; verifier/hook changes tree; mismatch refuses. **Does not prove tests ran.** |
| V-CRASH | Every WAL/pin/CAS/index/tree/receipt boundary; preserve binary/untracked/user edits and uncertain locks |
| V-ORDER | Node→addon→Rust→hook ladder, Rust scope seeded from passed held ranks, no hidden lower-rank scope, land request carrying 30 refused, no hook waiting, cancellation drains domain |
| V-BORROW | Existing alias/filter/hook/background/gc and sibling probes, parent death, no fd leak/text spoof |
| V-CUSTODY | `dispatch-cwd` lease held from producer start through runner commit and handoff; attempt exit→commit handoff; missing/stale/unverifiable lease fails closed; a never-locked description passed as a handle refused; lease borrowed into git children; successor/reset/remove races refused; after controller death with a live producer descendant (including a `setsid` one) the successor is refused with `custody-predecessor-unknown` unless the declared domain is proven empty; **state domain-empty assumption currently false** |
| V-SCOPE | Existing golden grammar through one implementation |
| V-TRUST | Poisoned GIT env/.git/hooks/fsmonitor/symlinks/refs; genuine hook temporary index retained |
| V-SYNC | Clean two-parent sync, scoped resolver, foreign abort, manual completion, no useless worker |
| V-CLEAN | Busy/dirty/orphan/relocate/native lock/CAS delete; no rm-rf fallback/data loss |
| V-CONSUMER | Real business-discussion read-only Unit route, including a detached HEAD without `origin/HEAD` and a directory that is not a repository (`notARepository`); labelled noncoding writing-land **fixture** |
| V-PACK | External pack/install/activate of the combined addon, manifest ABI/digests, rollback pending WAL, no PATH fallback, a second separate addon load fails closed |
| V-PARITY | Old consumer outputs/event timing, real merged OID, post-success settlement faults |
| V-GATE | Whole-product zero mutation exceptions by the §12 detection rule (static data-flow scan plus runtime spawn check), each of the nine missed shapes seeded as a false-negative probe; boundary row, no Node repair core, one matcher |

**Experiments** (falsifiers named; results of the 2026-10-18 runs are in §19):

- E1 power-loss / `core.fsync` on owner filesystems (objects, refs, **and** file WAL), including the directory fsyncs `fgos-git` itself performs (§10). SIGKILL ≠ this; tmpfs ≠ this. Needs root or device-mapper fault injection on a real device.
- E2 interrupted index/worktree/native-lock/registration with binary, untracked, symlink, mode, unrelated edits.
- E3 spawn/p50/p95/p99 **and** recovery backlog / disk amplification of the combined addon against the current Node writers; budgets are written down before the run, never invented after. Reversal trigger for Q1: the combined addon fails a stated budget or rollback with live processes. Workloads are reported separately: new object, existing object, no-op ref, changing ref, local transport with and without transferred objects, full typed operation; Node→addon calls and backend Git subprocess counts are reported separately. A CLI-only measurement is a baseline, not combined-addon acceptance. Backlog reports pending operations, records per operation, logical and allocated retained bytes, inode count, oldest debt, scan completeness and full reconciliation latency separately.
- E4 hooks/filters/background children, deadline, descendant drain **including `setsid()` and double-fork escapees**, through the Process containment driver of each platform (§5), not a process-group kill; Windows only after driver.
  2026-10-20 (phase 07 unit 22, owner decision): where the Process containment driver is `linux-pgroup` (no user namespace; `bwrap-pidns` hosts are unchanged), a borrowed operation (ScopedCommit, Sync, Land) never reports plain success while a `setsid`/double-fork descendant that dropped the borrowed descriptor is alive after the borrow's own cleanup: it returns `containment-unproven` as `recovery-required`, the effect stays in the WAL for recovery, and the escapee is not killed (it is unprovable by this driver). The rule is stated as a state, not a guess: when a hook or filter may run (an executable hook file in the effective hooks directory, an unreadable hooks directory, or any configured `filter.*.clean|smudge|process` command), the Git driver asks the borrow to run watched; the borrow plants a per-run marker in the child's environment, which every descendant inherits through reparenting, `setsid` and double fork, and after cleanup lists every live process of the user that still carries it. A failed run (non-zero exit) keeps its own failure result. Hook-free and filter-free operations are never watched and keep plain success. Residual (best-effort, as the `linux-pgroup` guarantee already says): a descendant that clears its own environment, or runs as another user, is not found; macOS `macos-tree` is not covered by this decision. 2026-10-20 (phase 07 unit 23, coordinator note): the shipped rule stays. A borrow parks only when a marked descendant survives it on `linux-pgroup`; it does not park whenever a hook ran. The literal "park whenever a hook ran" would park every hook-running commit on this machine and on the owner's, since both select `linux-pgroup` (`apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=1`). The residual gaps stay as stated: a descendant that runs `env -i` or runs as another user, an escapee behind a failing hook (which reports the hook's own failure), and `macos-tree`. 2026-10-21 (phase 07 unit 25, host gate): the watch no longer depends on the containment driver. A borrowed run is spawned by holder-lock with the descriptor mapped, outside every containment domain, so a `bwrap-pidns` machine is no better placed to see the escapee: the host gate machine selects `bwrap-pidns` (its AppArmor `bwrap` profile grants `userns`; only a confined agent session falls back to `linux-pgroup`) and the owning test reported plain `committed` there 3 of 3. The Git driver now asks for a watched borrow wherever a hook or filter may run, on every driver. 2026-10-22 (containment fixes, owner-approved council): contained (unborrowed) children are watched the same way on a best-effort driver (`linux-pgroup`). A pid namespace drains them on `bwrap-pidns`, so nothing changes there. The marker is planted when `user_code_may_run` holds or the repository has a submodule (a `.gitmodules` file in the worktree, or a non-empty `<common dir>/modules`): a superproject `status` runs a submodule's own clean filter, which the superproject's config check cannot see. A zero exit with a marked survivor returns `containment-unproven`, and the survivor is not killed. A non-zero exit keeps its own failure, as for borrows. `config` reads are never watched. Residual: off Linux there is no environment scan, so `macos-tree` contained children are not watched. On every driver, a refused `kill_all` after a deadline, an output limit or a root exit returns `containment-unproven` without waiting for the domain; it never hangs on a member that cannot die.
- E5 Git floor: `update-ref --stdin` transactions, `-m`, packed refs, SHA-256, hostile names, `refs/fgos/*` not in default fetch/push. Forge acceptance separate. **P-SYM-HOOK** on this floor, not man pages. Probe shallow clones, clone `--reference`/`--shared` alternates and promisor partial clones for every typed operation; record which are supported, degraded or refused.
- E6 classification of every assertion that reaches a Git write site as kept / retargeted / retired (§15 opening), over the regenerated suite; source-guard false-negative probes for the nine shapes of §12.
- P-SPACE ENOSPC/EIO/permission at prepare/object/pin/CAS/receipt/parent-dir sync, before **and after** the effect, on a real small filesystem (not a shared tmpfs).
- P-RELEASE live old process during activate/rollback; pending newer WAL refused by the activation door (§12); old `fgctl` stopped by the fence; stop-everything cutover for lock-free writers; external project; no legacy-writer fallback.
- P-CUTOVER authorized event-file deletion through the `cutoverGrant` of §7 (borrowed main 50 + WAL exact paths + `refs/fgos/legacy` OID); other tracked `.fgos` still refused, in the same commit and outside it; no env/PID grant; the Event Store ordering of §12 holds.
- P-RESOURCE: at pre-effect and post-effect barriers inject EMFILE/ENFILE, ENOMEM, spawn EAGAIN, output and queue saturation, and deadline cancellation. Required: bounded admission and output, every descriptor closed, WAL/pins/snapshots preserved, no success reported before durability, effect-aware receipts. Ceilings and workload are declared before measuring; a passing budget is never invented afterward.
- Second-load: a second, separate addon load of `fgos-event-store` or `fgos-git` in a Node process that already loaded the combined addon fails closed.

**Models** (bounded; fair storage / cooperative writers / supported containment are named assumptions):

- M1 CAS/recovery: two landers, crash every boundary, later advance, **equal-next OID**, **ABA**, same id / different digest, **cross-resource id**. The winner invariant is per WAL attempt and per uninterrupted expected-state generation, never per OID value over history (§2); each operation publishes at most once. File WAL vs pin disagreement must be **designed out**, not modeled as lawful. Amendment A1 extends M1 to two PublicationCas operations on one ref (§10 rule 4a): crash on both sides of the old target rename after its TargetAttempt, the old orphan git child still able to fire, ABA, equal and differing `next`; required: at most one publication per operation, `superseded` only after the later durable receipt, no attribution beyond WAL evidence; negative controls (supersede without the later receipt or on tip equality alone, supersede with a live old domain, supersede across a different authority, replay of the old CAS) must each give a counterexample.
- M2 ranks/borrow: Node 10/15/30, Git 20/40/50 in a Rust LockScope seeded from the caller's explicit held ranks and verified lease descriptors (option (a)), hook 55, seal 58, state 60, append 70; hook non-wait; no free lock while child writes. Checks the R30 table of §5, including the lease release before land and refusal of a land request that carries 30.
- M3 custody: `dispatch-cwd` lease extended across the runner commit and borrowed into git children; attempt exit, controller death, successor, commit retry; at most one authorized writer; domain-empty currently **false** on Linux/macOS.
- M4 local land success → consumer append fail → retry → cleanup: no second land, operation id preserved.
- P-WAL-ID one transition writer, by the no-overwrite record install of §10, including the loser's stop point and a recoverer that takes over a dead writer.
- P-CUSTODY overlapping writers, with the §6 admission rule.
- C1-C6 are checked separately, each with its own predicate and mapping, never as one label: C1 namespace/hooks by V-PUB and E5 (real backend); C2 by P-WAL-ID; C3 receipt/attribution by M1 and V-CRASH; C4 storage by P-SPACE and E1; C5 release by P-RELEASE; C6 env contexts and matcher by V-TRUST, V-SCOPE and V-SYNC.

V-REF-01 (Event Store, local bare remote) already showed plumbing CAS on `refs/fgos/*` has one winner among contenders, packed-ref/gc survival, and default fetch not carrying `refs/fgos/*`. That is Event Store evidence, **not** G0 V-PUB/C1; the Git floor run of 2026-10-18 covered hooks and symbolic refs on raw Git (§19).

## 16. Frozen catalogue: complete disposition

Every G-001..G-234 appears once. Frozen obligation text lives in `plans/reports/research-261017-1032-git-lessons-catalogue.md`; G0's **effective rule** is this section. Precedence: accepted Holder-lock/Event Store, then this G0 replacement (the 32-group override table below), then historical wording. A broad “Carry” is not a disposition when frozen text retains contradictory mechanics.

**Owner amendment 2026-10-20 11:34 — retirement timing.** The event-file dispositions below name the eventual WORK/Event Store cutover, not authorization to drop their assertions during Git caller migration. Until WORK cuts over, retain/retarget those guards through the temporary Work adapter of §§12–13; no event assertion is retired merely because Git phase 07 begins. The frozen catalogue remains unchanged.

**Retired event-file families (D-ADR0055).** Wholly retired: G-089, G-090, G-093, G-118, G-120, G-121, G-170, G-210, G-211, G-212, G-214. Partially retired, with retained non-event obligations: G-029 (event workaround gone; worker `.fgos` strip retained), G-092 (event-file aspect retired; interrupted-merge and native-lock recovery stay in §§9–11, and doctor keeps the detect-only, ownership-aware MERGE_HEAD rule), G-181 (no event-shard stage; Git never writes `.fgos` state payload), G-184 (no production guard-bypass switch), G-213 (event-file guard retired; typed guard-failure diagnostics with a stderr record, an explicit ceiling or streaming for every large blob read, and the rule that a committed-read error never reads as 0 stay in §§2/7, V-TRUST/V-PARITY). G-206 is partially retired by the RT2-F1 override below. Keep G-054/069/086/088/166 (non-event `.fgos`) and all remaining non-event tracked `.fgos` safeguards. Remove old event guards only with actual Event Store cutover. Legacy TTL/PID/heartbeat/unlink lock mechanisms are superseded by unchanged 4.3-R, not recreated in Rust.

**C6 and critique overrides (replace “Carry” on these rows).** 32 row groups: the 14 original groups, 9 added by amendment A1 (23 in all), and 9 added per red-team finding RT2-F1, confirmed by the owner on 2026-10-17. In Appendix A the Disposition and obligation cells of every row in these groups are replaced by the effective rule below; the old frozen text is not repeated next to it and stays only in the catalogue file.

| Rows | Effective rule |
|---|---|
| G-001 / G-183 | Two named env contexts, one backend owner: scrubbed runner; validated hook-index context. |
| `G-027` | Work owns claim reclaim **policy**. Git supplies facts (status, mtimes, branch tips) only. Do not claim reclaim in a Git facts row. |
| G-030 / G-031 | **Execution**, after `worktree-create` returns, outside every Git lock. Git does not spawn `npm` or setup commands. |
| `G-042` | Require dirty-check through destroy under one ownership: worktree-admin 40 wraps add, remove, relocate and prune (holder-lock §16 amended 2026-10-17, owner decision Q6). |
| `G-046` | Guarded cleanup; **no** `finally` force-remove / rm-rf. |
| `G-062` | ScopedCommit and Sync share one commit-failure classification: preserve original commit stderr verbatim; replay only the RepositoryConfig-owned safe guard through Git; never replay a foreign hook. |
| G-071 vs old A §8 | Temporary resolver grant for this merge's scopable conflict paths only; `writeScope` never permanently expands. |
| `G-080` | Consumer verifies; Git binds evidence digest + tree identity; Git does not claim tests ran. Destination never contains an unpublished unverified **Git** merge of an unbound tree. |
| G-166 / G-223 event-file | Event-file deletion only in authorized exact cutover grant after legacy import/verification; retain all other `.fgos` protections. |
| `G-193` | Preserve bytes and session identity; legacy force is not a data-loss bypass. |
| `G-202` | Frozen by owner decision Q5: catchup uses the isolated scoped resolver iff custody; else abort own merge. Land aborts. Catalogue Q16 closed by the owner. |
| G-216 / G-122–G-134 | Kernel Holder-lock supersedes TTL/PID/unlink. Safety lesson (fail-closed ambiguous, no force) retained; mechanism not recreated. |
| G-223 PID-grant / truncation steps | Superseded by D-ADR0055 and Holder-lock borrow verification. Preserve ordering of **surviving** guards. |
| `G-234` | Consumer owns Iron Law/approval; Git binds OIDs/target/evidence. |
| `G-059` | Commit subject and message are consumer-supplied through the ScopedCommit intent; Git validates encoding and length limits and never formats conventional-commit subjects. |
| `G-081` | Workflow's integrate path is deleted in the same release (§13); no best-effort `worktree remove --force`, `rm -rf` fallback or `branch -D` survives; the declared integrate target resolution rule (declared step, else `origin/HEAD`, else checked-out branch) moves to the consumer-side Land intent. |
| G-098 / G-099 / G-102 | Carried unchanged and made explicit in §9 steps 4-5: own-file-set clean gate with whole-repo and subtree scopes, `.fgos` never counts, fail-safe when the set is absent; second check under main 50 immediately before CAS returns `main-checkout-dirty-mid-merge` without moving the ref; after CAS, `read-tree -m -u <old destination> <candidate>` only when the main checkout holds the target, tolerating non-overlapping dirty paths per D-ADR0042 and reporting a lagging checkout in the receipt. |
| `G-100` | Dependency provisioning and setup commands run by Execution after worktree-create and outside every Git lock (same rule as G-030/G-031); a provisioning failure is a consumer verify-fail and never touches main. |
| `G-113` | Ownership re-verification before the critical action is retained; synchronous renew and heartbeat ticks are superseded by unchanged 4.3-R kernel locks (lock-lost-mid-merge is reported from a failed kernel re-check, never from a TTL). |
| G-135 / G-136 / G-137 / G-138 / G-139 / G-140 | v1 `protocol.json` markers, `allowSelfRecognition`, release-if-own unlink, timestamp renew and heartbeat timers are superseded by unchanged Holder-lock; retained lessons: fail closed on unsupported or ambiguous lock state, never unlink, stop-the-world cutover with tombstone. |
| G-161 / G-162 | Hook recognises an fgOS commit only through Holder-lock borrow verification (`run_borrowed`, `FGOS_LOCK_FD` mapped slot); `FGOS_MAIN_LOCK_HOLDER_PID` integer-PID grants are superseded; hook probes once and never waits. |
| `G-175` | Landing and cleanup stay separate phases; branch deletion uses expected-tip CAS deleteRef (§3/§11), never `branch -D`; delivery proof by hierarchical ancestry and TTL anchored at delivered are retained. |
| `G-194` | Existing guards retained (self-mount realpath, HEAD differs from startCommit, status fail-closed); the final step is guarded non-destructive remove with prune, never `worktree remove --force`; one cleanup policy source (G-041). |
| G-032 (override per RT2-F1) | Git worktree-create ends at registration and the worker `.fgos` strip, then returns; it takes no `beforeProvision` callback. Execution provisions dependencies and runs setup after create returns, outside every Git lock (§1, G-030/G-031). Retained lesson: a lock is held only for the narrow Git metadata mutation, and nothing holds main while provisioning runs. |
| G-033 (override per RT2-F1) | Git rolls back only its own create effects: before registration it removes only the operation's own mkdtemp directory; after registration a failed `.fgos` strip unregisters and removes through the guarded non-destructive cleanup of §11 (never `--force`, never `rm -rf`) and rethrows the original cause unmasked. Rollback after a provisioning or setup failure belongs to Execution, which calls the guarded Worktree remove; releasing the runtime claim on create failure is Work/Execution policy. |
| G-095 (override per RT2-F1) | Execution runs the item's verify string through Process in the candidate worktree, outside Git and outside every Git lock (§1, §9). Consumer-side lessons retained: only exit status decides; timeout is configurable (`timeoutMs`), resolves as a typed result and stays distinct from a code failure; stdin ignored; a 10 MiB output ceiling; one configured threshold for every verify path. Git binds the resulting evidence digest and tree identity and never runs verify. |
| G-116 (override per RT2-F1) | Holder-lock kernel open-file-description locks release when the holding descriptor closes, including on process exit; no per-call `releaseOnExit` signal listener is installed. The library never takes over process termination (no `process.exit`) and installs no process-wide handler that kills Node (§5). Release stays an explicit close in `finally`. |
| G-141 / G-142 / G-143 / G-144 / G-145 / G-146 / G-147 (override per RT2-F1) | Lock identity, waiting and holding follow unchanged 4.3-R Holder-lock: registry paths with `dispatch-cwd` keyed by dev:ino after realpath, not v1 `encodeURIComponent` file names; one merge-slot 20 per target ref, so different targets never contend and the slot never replaces CAS; held is retryable named contention, ambiguous or unsupported state fails closed and is never retried, and held asserts nothing about liveness; waits use a monotonic bounded `waitMs` budget covering all retries, with no TTL-derived budget and no 250 ms grace sleep, retrying only the acquire before mutation; verify runs outside every Git lock; no non-slot merge holds main-checkout 50 across merge, verify and commit, and no heartbeat exists; main 50 is taken only for the short main index/tree/HEAD mutation of §9 steps 4-5. Proof obligation retained: a same-target process stays refused for the whole verify while the slot is held (V-LAND, V-ORDER). |
| G-158 (override per RT2-F1) | Superseded by landed 4.4a/4.3-R Holder-lock: `fgos-git` writes no acquire, renew, release, composite-identity, TTL, slot or beacon code in distribution's `lock.rs` or anywhere else, and uses Holder-lock unchanged. Retained lesson: lock constants have one source, checked by Holder-lock's existing tests. |
| G-191 (override per RT2-F1) | Session worktree creation keeps the detached mkdtemp path, the `sessionId` character check before touching any path, realpath nesting refusal and the never-throwing `isSessionWorktree`. Rollback after a failure uses the guarded non-destructive Worktree remove with prune (§11), never `worktree remove --force` and never `rm -rf`; an uncertain state is preserved and reported as recovery-required. Whether this door merges with worktree-create is a migration decision (§13). |
| G-195 (override per RT2-F1) | Every worktree-creating door (claim, dispatch, merge, session, return, workflow, mutation script) goes through one Git create/cleanup operation: `.fgos` strip (G-029), prune after remove, guarded cleanup on failure (G-033, G-041), and serialization under worktree-admin 40, which wraps add, remove, relocate and prune (G-155; holder-lock §16 as amended for Q6). Dependency provisioning (G-030) and setup (G-031) are run by Execution after create returns, outside every Git lock, not by Git. |
| G-206 (override per RT2-F1) | Partial retirement under D-ADR0055: the dispatch attestation guard reads the unified Event Store view, not baseline `events.jsonl` plus `.fgos/events/` writer shards. Retained lesson: "no event found" is distinguished from "valid in-session path" by an explicit signal, never by silence, and every event read goes through one unified reader (Event Store). |
